11. Core Schema and Core Types

11.1 esdb-core

StreamId

#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq, Hash, PartialOrd, Ord)]
pub struct StreamId(Arc<str>);

impl StreamId {
    pub fn new(s: impl Into<Arc<str>>) -> Result<Self, InvalidStreamId>;
    pub fn as_str(&self) -> &str;
    pub fn category(&self) -> &str;          // prefix before first '-'; invariant: non-empty
    pub fn is_system(&self) -> bool;         // starts with '$'
}
Property Semantics
Ownership Arc<str>; clone is O(1) ref-bump
Mutation immutable after construction
Concurrency Send + Sync; share freely across threads
Persistence serialized as raw UTF-8 string; max 512 bytes
Compatibility schema version-independent; stored verbatim
Invariants non-empty; no whitespace; no control chars; I2 relies on uniqueness

Category derivation: category = s[..first_dash_pos]; if no - then category == full id.

EventId

#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq, Hash)]
pub struct EventId(pub Uuid);

impl EventId {
    pub fn new_v7() -> Self;               // time-ordered UUIDv7; preferred
    pub fn from_uuid(u: Uuid) -> Self;
}
Property Semantics
Ownership Copy; 16 bytes on stack
Mutation immutable
Concurrency Send + Sync
Persistence 16-byte big-endian; also displayable as hyphenated UUID string
Compatibility idempotency key (I6); must survive round-trip without truncation
Invariants globally unique; never reused; UUIDv7 recommended for sortability

EventNumber

#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq, Hash, PartialOrd, Ord)]
pub struct EventNumber(pub u64);

impl EventNumber {
    pub const START: Self = EventNumber(0);
    pub fn next(self) -> Self;             // wrapping-add would be a bug; panic in debug
    pub fn checked_next(self) -> Option<Self>;
}
Property Semantics
Ownership Copy; 8 bytes
Mutation immutable per event; stream head advances only forward
Concurrency compare-and-swap in esdb-stream for optimistic concurrency (I5)
Persistence stored as u64 LE in index entries
Compatibility gap-free within a stream (I2); no reuse after tombstone

GlobalPosition

#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq, Hash, PartialOrd, Ord)]
pub struct GlobalPosition(pub u64);

impl GlobalPosition {
    pub const START: Self = GlobalPosition(0);
    pub fn as_u64(self) -> u64;
}
Property Semantics
Ownership Copy; 8 bytes
Mutation immutable per event; global counter advances only on commit
Concurrency assigned under leader lock in Raft path; unique and monotonic (I3)
Persistence stored as u64 LE; primary cursor for $all, subscriptions, projections
Compatibility cursor for all external consumers; never reused or reordered

ExpectedVersion

#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub enum ExpectedVersion {
    Any,                       // disable concurrency check; always append
    NoStream,                  // stream must not exist (current version == -1)
    StreamExists,              // stream must exist; any version acceptable
    Exact(EventNumber),        // stream's current last event number must match
}
Variant Guard semantics
Any always appends; no version check
NoStream rejects if stream has any events; used for "create-once" aggregates
StreamExists rejects if stream has no events; used for "update-only" writes
Exact(n) rejects if last EventNumber != n; the standard optimistic-concurrency path

Mutation: immutable enum; passed by value. Error: mismatch yields WrongExpectedVersion { stream, expected, actual }.

ReadDirection

#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub enum ReadDirection { Forward, Backward }

pub struct ReadResult<T> {
    pub events: Vec<T>,
    pub next_position: Option<T>,   // None when is_end
    pub is_end: bool,
}

T is EventRecord in stream reads, GlobalPosition in $all reads. ReadResult is non-mutating; caller owns the Vec.

AppendResult

#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy)]
pub struct AppendResult {
    pub next_expected_version: EventNumber,   // last written event number
    pub commit_position: GlobalPosition,      // position of last event in $all
}

Returned synchronously after quorum-ack (Phase 10+). Pre-replication: commit_position reflects local log.

11.2 esdb-event

EventRecord

#[derive(Debug, Clone)]
pub struct EventRecord {
    pub event_id:    EventId,
    pub stream:      StreamId,
    pub number:      EventNumber,
    pub event_type:  Arc<str>,           // domain type string; e.g. "OrderPlaced"
    pub data:        Bytes,              // opaque; store never parses (P10 / ADR-0010)
    pub metadata:    Bytes,              // causation-id, correlation-id, custom fields
    pub content_type: ContentType,       // JSON | Binary | custom MIME
    pub position:    GlobalPosition,     // assigned on commit; zero before commit
    pub created:     SystemTime,         // wall-clock; approximate; not a sort key
}

#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub enum ContentType { Json, Binary, Custom([u8; 32]) }
Property Semantics
Ownership Bytes fields are ref-counted (bytes::Bytes); clone is O(1)
Mutation I1: all fields immutable after commit; pre-commit position is sentinel 0
Concurrency Send + Sync; safe to share across projection/subscription threads
Persistence framed as: [u32 len][payload][u32 CRC]; payload = protobuf EventRecord wire
Compatibility event_type is the versioning key; UpcastFn registry in esdb-event handles schema migration
Invariants I1: id/stream/number/position/data never mutate post-commit

UpcastFn trait (esdb-event/src/versioning.rs):

pub trait UpcastFn: Send + Sync {
    fn event_type(&self) -> &str;
    fn from_version(&self) -> u32;
    fn to_version(&self) -> u32;
    fn upcast(&self, data: Bytes) -> Result<Bytes, UpcastError>;
}

11.3 esdb-log

Segment

pub struct Segment {
    pub id:         u64,               // monotonic segment sequence number
    pub path:       PathBuf,
    pub base_offset: u64,              // first record's byte offset in this file
    pub size:       u64,               // current written bytes
}

pub const SEGMENT_MAX_BYTES: u64 = 256 * 1024 * 1024;  // 256 MiB; configurable
pub const RECORD_HEADER_LEN: usize = 8;                  // 4-byte len + 4-byte CRC
Property Semantics
Mutation append-only; size advances on write; no in-place edit
Persistence one file per segment; name = {id:020}.seg; fsync on roll
Recovery I8/P8: scan last segment from known-good offset; truncate at first CRC mismatch

IndexEntry

#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy)]
pub struct IndexEntry {
    pub stream_hash: u64,          // FNV-1a of StreamId bytes
    pub number:      EventNumber,
    pub offset:      u64,          // byte offset in segment file
    pub position:    GlobalPosition,
}

Stored in a separate .idx file per segment; rebuilt from log on recovery. Two index views: (stream_hash, number) -> offset and position -> offset.

11.4 esdb-stream

StreamEngine (trait)

pub trait StreamEngine: Send + Sync {
    fn append(
        &self,
        stream: &StreamId,
        expected: ExpectedVersion,
        events: Vec<NewEvent>,
    ) -> Result<AppendResult, AppendError>;

    fn read(
        &self,
        stream: &StreamId,
        from: EventNumber,
        count: usize,
        dir: ReadDirection,
    ) -> Result<ReadResult<EventRecord>, ReadError>;

    fn tombstone(&self, stream: &StreamId, expected: ExpectedVersion)
        -> Result<(), AppendError>;
}

pub struct NewEvent {
    pub event_id:    EventId,
    pub event_type:  Arc<str>,
    pub data:        Bytes,
    pub metadata:    Bytes,
    pub content_type: ContentType,
}

Mutation: append is the only write path; atomicity = all-or-nothing (I4). Concurrency: internal RwLock per stream (or per-shard); ExpectedVersion::Exact CAS on stream head. WrongExpectedVersion is returned, not panicked; caller must retry or propagate.

11.5 esdb-snapshot

Snapshot

#[derive(Debug, Clone)]
pub struct Snapshot {
    pub stream:        StreamId,
    pub version:       EventNumber,      // stream version at snapshot time
    pub state:         Bytes,            // serialized aggregate state; opaque
    pub position:      GlobalPosition,   // $all position at snapshot time
    pub schema_version: u32,             // invalidated if projection schema changes
    pub created:       SystemTime,
}

SnapshotStore (trait)

pub trait SnapshotStore: Send + Sync {
    fn write(&self, snap: Snapshot) -> Result<(), SnapshotError>;
    fn load_nearest(
        &self,
        stream: &StreamId,
        at_or_before: EventNumber,
    ) -> Result<Option<Snapshot>, SnapshotError>;
    fn invalidate(&self, stream: &StreamId, schema_version: u32)
        -> Result<u64, SnapshotError>;   // returns count invalidated
}

Invariant I9: load_nearest(stream, N) + replay (snap.version+1)..=N == fold full stream to N.

11.6 esdb-projection

Projection

#[derive(Debug, Clone)]
pub struct Projection {
    pub name:       Arc<str>,
    pub query:      Arc<str>,               // DSL or handler registration token
    pub checkpoint: Option<GlobalPosition>, // None = never checkpointed
    pub state:      Bytes,                  // opaque; handler-defined
    pub mode:       ProjectionMode,
    pub status:     ProjectionStatus,
}

#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub enum ProjectionMode { Continuous, OneTime }

#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub enum ProjectionStatus { Running, Paused, Faulted, Rebuilding }

ProjectionHandler (trait)

pub trait ProjectionHandler: Send + Sync {
    fn event_types(&self) -> &[&str];          // filter; empty = all types
    fn apply(
        &self,
        state: &mut dyn Any,
        event: &EventRecord,
    ) -> Result<(), ProjectionError>;          // must be deterministic (I8)
    fn initial_state(&self) -> Box<dyn Any + Send>;
}

Checkpoint: written after each successfully applied batch; position = event.position. Rebuild = reset checkpoint to GlobalPosition::START + re-run from 0 (P9).

11.7 esdb-subscribe

Subscription

#[derive(Debug, Clone)]
pub struct Subscription {
    pub id:     Uuid,
    pub filter: SubscriptionFilter,
    pub from:   SubscriptionPosition,
    pub kind:   SubscriptionKind,
}

#[derive(Debug, Clone)]
pub enum SubscriptionFilter {
    All,
    Stream(StreamId),
    Category(Arc<str>),
    EventType(Arc<str>),
}

#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy)]
pub enum SubscriptionPosition {
    Start,
    End,
    After(GlobalPosition),
}

#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub enum SubscriptionKind { Volatile, Persistent }

Checkpoint

#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy)]
pub struct Checkpoint {
    pub subscription_id: Uuid,
    pub position:        GlobalPosition,
    pub committed_at:    SystemTime,
}

Persistence: checkpoints written to esdb-storage; fsynced. Lost checkpoint = replay from last known position; at-least-once then idempotency yields exactly-once effect (P5).

Competing consumer semantics:

Field Semantics
group_name identifies the persistent subscription group
ack(event_id) marks event processed; advances group checkpoint
nack(event_id, action) action ∈ {Park, Retry, Skip, Stop}
max_retry_count before parking to dead-letter stream
consumer_strategy RoundRobin | Pinned | DispatchToSingle

11.8 esdb-replication

ReplicationEntry

#[derive(Debug, Clone)]
pub struct ReplicationEntry {
    pub position: GlobalPosition,       // the assigned $all position
    pub term:     u64,                  // Raft term in which leader appended this
    pub record:   EventRecord,          // the committed event
}

ClusterNode

#[derive(Debug, Clone)]
pub struct ClusterNode {
    pub node_id:      NodeId,               // stable UUID
    pub addr:         SocketAddr,
    pub role:         NodeRole,
    pub term:         u64,
    pub commit_index: GlobalPosition,       // highest committed position known
    pub match_index:  GlobalPosition,       // highest position acked to leader (leader view)
    pub last_contact: SystemTime,
}

#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub enum NodeRole { Leader, Follower, Candidate, PreCandidate }

Invariant I7: commit_index advances only when a quorum of match_index values reach or exceed the position. A committed position is never rolled back.

RaftTransport (trait)

pub trait RaftTransport: Send + Sync {
    async fn send_append_entries(
        &self, target: NodeId, req: AppendEntriesRequest,
    ) -> Result<AppendEntriesResponse, TransportError>;

    async fn send_vote_request(
        &self, target: NodeId, req: VoteRequest,
    ) -> Result<VoteResponse, TransportError>;
}

11.9 esdb-partition

StreamRouter (trait)

pub trait StreamRouter: Send + Sync {
    fn owner_of(&self, stream: &StreamId) -> NodeId;
    fn is_local(&self, stream: &StreamId) -> bool;
    fn all_owners(&self) -> Vec<(NodeId, Vec<StreamId>)>;
}

ScavengePlan

#[derive(Debug, Clone)]
pub struct ScavengePlan {
    pub segments:    Vec<SegmentPlan>,
    pub initiated_at: SystemTime,
    pub initiated_by: Arc<str>,    // operator identity
}

#[derive(Debug, Clone)]
pub struct SegmentPlan {
    pub segment_id: u64,
    pub live_ranges: Vec<Range<u64>>,   // byte ranges to preserve
    pub bytes_to_remove: u64,
}

Invariant I10: live_ranges covers all events not in {deleted ∪ expired ∪ over-maxcount}. Scavenge must not alter EventNumber or GlobalPosition of surviving events.

11.10 Type Compatibility and Evolution Rules

Rule Detail
EventRecord wire format protobuf-encoded; new optional fields are backward-compatible; required fields never removed
EventNumber / GlobalPosition u64; max ~1.8×10¹⁹; no planned overflow; saturating arithmetic would be a fatal bug
StreamId encoding UTF-8; normalized to NFC before storage; comparison is byte-equal after normalization
ExpectedVersion enum variants are stable; new variants are additive only (old clients use Any fallback)
Snapshot schema_version bumped on every projection handler schema change; old snapshots auto-invalidated
Checkpoint durable format (subscription_id UUID, position u64) stored as 24-byte fixed record; no versioning needed
Segment file format magic 0xESD800 + 1-byte format version; version mismatch on open is a hard error
Raft log entry protobuf ReplicationEntry; term/position fields never reordered; new fields appended only
API protobuf contracts streams.proto / projections.proto follow protobuf field-number stability rules; no field renumber
Cross-crate dep direction esdb-core ← esdb-event ← esdb-log ← esdb-stream ← {esdb-snapshot, esdb-subscribe, esdb-projection} ← esdb-replication ← esdb-partition ← esdb-storage ← esdb-server; esdb-cli depends on esdb-server only; no upward deps

Phase 1 — core types + event model + positions

1.1 Objectives

  • Establish esdb-core and esdb-event as zero-I/O foundational crates.
  • Define all canonical identity and versioning types: StreamId, EventId, EventNumber, GlobalPosition.
  • Define EventRecord, ExpectedVersion, AppendResult, ReadDirection, Snapshot, Checkpoint.
  • Fix serialization framing (wire format / storage envelope) for EventRecord used by all downstream crates.
  • Implement category derivation: StreamId prefix before first -.
  • Property-test round-trip: EventRecord serialize → deserialize → equality.
  • No I/O, no network, no filesystem touches anywhere in this phase.

1.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

<!-- Theory anchors: P3 (optimistic concurrency — ExpectedVersion), I2 (gap-free EventNumber), I6 (EventId idempotency key = RFC 4122/S4/S5), B2 (DDD aggregate-as-stream), S3 (protobuf framing), ADR-06 (custom length-prefix + CRC) -->

esdb-core/src/lib.rs — identity + version types

Type Shape Constraints
StreamId newtype(Arc<str>) non-empty; category = chars before first - or whole string
EventId newtype(Uuid) v4 or v7; globally unique idempotency key
EventNumber newtype(u64) 0-based; u64::MAX reserved as sentinel
GlobalPosition newtype(u64) strictly monotonic; 0 = pre-genesis sentinel
ExpectedVersion enum: Any | NoStream | StreamExists | Exact(EventNumber) wire-stable discriminants
ReadDirection enum: Forward | Backward
AppendResult struct: { next_expected_version: EventNumber, commit_position: GlobalPosition }

StreamId::category() -> &str — slice before first - (whole id if absent); StreamId::validate(s: &str) -> Result<StreamId, ValidationError> — rejects empty, NUL, /.

esdb-event/src/lib.rs — event record + metadata

pub struct EventRecord {
    pub event_id:   EventId,
    pub stream:     StreamId,
    pub number:     EventNumber,
    pub event_type: Arc<str>,       // e.g. "OrderPlaced"
    pub content_type: ContentType,  // Json | Binary | Text
    pub data:       Bytes,
    pub metadata:   Bytes,
    pub position:   GlobalPosition,
    pub created:    i64,            // Unix micros, UTC
}
  • ContentType: enum Json | Binary | Text, stored as a 1-byte discriminant; EventType versioning is an opaque Arc<str> with convention "<type>:v<N>", not enforced by core.
  • Serialization framing (used by esdb-log): length-prefixed envelope — 4-byte LE payload length, then protobuf/MessagePack payload, then 4-byte CRC32C. Wire schema versioned with a 1-byte magic + version prefix.
  • Snapshot: { stream: StreamId, version: EventNumber, state: Bytes, position: GlobalPosition }.
  • Checkpoint: { id: Arc<str>, position: GlobalPosition }.

Property tests (esdb-event/tests/prop_roundtrip.rs)

  • Use proptest + quickcheck: generate arbitrary EventRecord → serialize → deserialize → assert field equality.
  • Include adversarial corpus: empty data, max-size data (1 MiB), non-UTF-8 in metadata.

1.3 Deliverable

  • esdb-core and esdb-event compile with zero warnings under cargo check --all-features.
  • All unit + property tests pass: cargo test -p esdb-core -p esdb-event.
  • EventRecord framing document (inline in esdb-event/src/framing.rs) pinning magic byte and schema version.
  • No I/O, no tokio, no std::fs imports anywhere in these crates.

1.4 Acceptance Criteria

Check Target
cargo test -p esdb-core -p esdb-event 100% pass
Proptest round-trip (10 000 cases) zero failures
StreamId::category() unit tests 8 cases, all correct
ExpectedVersion serde round-trip all 4 variants
EventRecord serialized size (empty payload) <= 64 bytes
Zero unsafe in these crates enforced by #![forbid(unsafe_code)]

1.5 Conformance Fixtures

File Purpose Expected outcome
fixtures/event_roundtrip_basic.bin canonical EventRecord binary deserialize == original
fixtures/event_roundtrip_empty_payload.bin zero-byte data + metadata size <= 64 bytes
fixtures/event_roundtrip_large.bin 1 MiB data field correct CRC, no truncation
fixtures/stream_id_categories.json 20 stream-id → category pairs all match category()
fixtures/expected_version_wire.json 4 variant encodings serde matches literals
fixtures/malformed_short_frame.bin truncated envelope (3 bytes) FrameError::TooShort
fixtures/malformed_bad_crc.bin flipped CRC byte FrameError::CrcMismatch

1.6 Pitfalls

Pitfall Manifestation Root cause Debug signal
Category derivation on streams with no - category() returns full id; callers that expect a prefix fail silently spec says "whole string" — easy to implement wrong Unit test with "nohyphen" input
GlobalPosition(0) as genesis vs. valid position Off-by-one in subscription cursors 0 used as both "start" and "first event" Reserve 0 explicitly; first event at 1
EventId collision in tests Idempotency dedup masks test bugs All test events share a hardcoded UUID Generate fresh UUIDs per test case
CRC computed over wrong byte range Corrupt-data reads succeed validation CRC includes framing length bytes Fix CRC scope in framing doc and test
ContentType discriminant drift Cross-version deserialization misroutes Enum reordering Pin #[repr(u8)] + wire value constants

1.7 Performance Concerns

  • Arc<str> for StreamId / event_type: zero-copy clone; intern hot streams with DashMap<str, StreamId>.
  • Bytes for data / metadata: shared-ownership, no copy on clone; use Bytes::copy_from_slice only once at ingestion.
  • Protobuf schema: prefer bytes fields (no UTF-8 check) for data and metadata.
  • Avoid Box<dyn Error> in hot paths; use a concrete error enum to stay on stack.
  • EventRecord struct layout: place Bytes fields last to minimize padding; target <= 128 bytes.

1.8 Edge Cases

  • StreamId with multiple -: category is prefix before first - only.
  • EventType empty string: reject at validation boundary; not allowed by spec.
  • EventNumber at u64::MAX - 1: must not overflow when incrementing; return StreamFull error.
  • GlobalPosition wrap-around: u64 range (~1.8 × 10¹⁹), documented as unreachable in practice but asserted on increment; created timestamp in the past or future (clock skew) is stored as provided and never validated — consumers must not sort by created.
  • Metadata Bytes exceeding 64 KiB: warn in metrics; not a hard error at this layer.

1.9 Failure Analysis

  • Bad framing magic byte: FrameError::UnknownMagic — blast radius: single record unreadable; log position + hex dump; recovery: skip or abort depending on caller policy.
  • CRC mismatch: FrameError::CrcMismatch — indicates storage or memory corruption; propagate to esdb-log crash-recovery path which truncates to last good record.
  • Type ID collision (two event types with same string): downstream projections route incorrectly, no core-layer defense, mitigated by registry conventions. Protobuf schema evolution: adding required fields breaks old decoders — use optional/proto3 only; test against old fixture binaries.

1.10 Operational Notes

  • EventRecord.event_id is the idempotency key — log at TRACE on every deserialize with id + stream + number.
  • Metrics (registered via metrics crate): esdb_core_validation_errors_total{reason}.
  • No deployment implications (pure library); changes require semver bump on esdb-core when wire format changes.
  • Framing version byte enables future zero-downtime format migration: new writer, old reader falls back by version dispatch.

Phase 2 — append-only segment log + index

2.1 Objectives

  • Implement esdb-log: the durable, append-only, crash-safe log underlying both per-stream and global logs.
  • Fixed-size segment files; length-prefixed + CRC records; fsync on commit; segment roll-over at size threshold.
  • Build dual indexes: (StreamId, EventNumber) → file offset and GlobalPosition → file offset.
  • Sequential append path; sequential and random read paths.
  • Crash recovery: scan last segment, detect torn write by CRC/length mismatch, truncate tail, rebuild in-memory index from last good position.
  • Establish WAL ordering: write-then-fsync before acknowledging.

2.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

<!-- Theory anchors: P3 (Gray & Reuter WAL), P5 (Kafka segment roll-over), R7 (RocksDB log reader crash-recovery), S6 (CRC-32C polynomial), S7 (POSIX fsync), P8 (Crash-Only Recovery principle), I4 (all-or-nothing append atomicity), ADR-09 (custom segment format) -->

Segment file format

segment_<seq>.log
  [record]*
record := | 4B LE length | payload (EventRecord framing from Phase 1) | 4B CRC32C |
  • Segment sequence: zero-padded 10-digit decimal in filename; monotonically increasing.
  • MAX_SEGMENT_SIZE: configurable; default 256 MiB; roll over when next append would exceed.
  • fsync(fd) after every commit (or batched per SyncPolicy: PerWrite | Periodic(Duration) | Never).

esdb-log/src/segment.rs

  • Segment { seq: u64, path: PathBuf, file: File, write_offset: u64 }.
  • Segment::append(encoded: &[u8]) -> Result<u64, LogError> — returns start offset; writes length + payload + CRC; updates write_offset.
  • Segment::read_at(offset: u64) -> Result<Bytes, LogError> — pread, validate length + CRC, return payload bytes.
  • Segment::scan_from(offset: u64) -> impl Iterator<Item=Result<(u64, Bytes), LogError>> — forward scan; stops on CRC error.

esdb-log/src/index.rs

  • StreamIndex: HashMap<(StreamId, EventNumber), (SegmentSeq, u64)> — in-memory; persisted as append-only index file.
  • PositionIndex: BTreeMap<GlobalPosition, (SegmentSeq, u64)> — for $all ordered reads.
  • Index file format: fixed 32-byte records [stream_hash(8) | event_number(8) | seg_seq(8) | offset(8)]; position index separate file [position(8) | seg_seq(8) | offset(8) | _pad(8)].
  • IndexWriter::flush() — appends new index entries, fsyncs index file.

esdb-log/src/log.rs — Log struct

  • Fields: segments: Vec<Segment>, stream_index: StreamIndex, position_index: PositionIndex, active_segment: Segment.
  • Log::append(record: &EventRecord) -> Result<u64, LogError> — encode via Phase 1 framing, write to active segment, update both indexes, fsync.
  • Log::read_stream(stream: &StreamId, from: EventNumber, dir: ReadDirection, limit: usize) -> Result<Vec<EventRecord>> — index lookup → pread.
  • Log::read_all(from: GlobalPosition, dir: ReadDirection, limit: usize) -> Result<Vec<EventRecord>> — position index walk.
  • Log::recover(dir: &Path) -> Result<Log, LogError> — open all segments by seq order; scan last segment from last known-good offset; on CRC error truncate with ftruncate; rebuild in-memory indexes from surviving records.

esdb-log/src/wal.rs

  • Sequence: encode → write → fsync → update index → return offset. No return before fsync.
  • Batch path: accumulate N records in BufWriter; single fsync at batch boundary; report per-record offsets.

2.3 Deliverable

  • esdb-log crate compiles and passes all tests.
  • Log::append + Log::read_stream + Log::read_all callable from integration test.
  • Crash simulation test: kill process mid-write, re-open, verify no torn record visible.
  • Segment roll-over verified: writing past MAX_SEGMENT_SIZE creates new segment file.
  • Index rebuild verified: delete in-memory state, reopen, all reads return same data.

2.4 Acceptance Criteria

Check Target
Unit tests cargo test -p esdb-log 100% pass
append throughput (256 B records, batched fsync) >= 100 K events/s on NVMe
read_stream (cold, random pread) <= 200 µs p99 per record
Crash recovery: truncate torn tail zero corrupt records after recovery
Segment roll-over at MAX_SEGMENT_SIZE new segment created; old sealed
Index rebuild from empty in-memory state all appended records readable
CRC mismatch on read LogError::CrcMismatch returned, not panic

2.5 Conformance Fixtures

File Purpose Expected outcome
fixtures/log/single_segment_10.bin 10 records, single segment all 10 readable in order
fixtures/log/multi_segment_rollover.bin log crossing segment boundary reads span segments correctly
fixtures/log/torn_write_tail.bin last record incomplete (truncated mid-payload) recovery truncates; 9 of 10 readable
fixtures/log/bad_crc_middle.bin record 5 of 10 has flipped CRC byte CrcMismatch on record 5; others readable
fixtures/log/index_rebuild_golden.bin segment + empty index files rebuild matches original in-memory index
fixtures/log/large_record_1mib.bin 1 MiB payload record reads back exactly; no off-by-one on length

2.6 Pitfalls

Pitfall Manifestation Root cause Debug signal
fsync omitted on segment file descriptor data loss on crash; appears fine in tests File::write returns OK without durable flush Trace fsync calls; chaos test with kill -9
Index not fsynced before segment fsync index points to position before new records index write reordered by OS fsync index file after segment
Off-by-one in scan_from start offset first record at offset 0 skipped or double-read confusion between write_offset and record start Fixture: single record at offset 0
ftruncate to wrong offset deletes good records or leaves corrupt tail recovery uses stale write_offset Assert truncated offset == last good record end
HashMap key uses StreamId Arc<str> by pointer different Arc instances with same content miss pointer equality vs string equality Use Eq impl on StreamId by content
Segment fd left open after seal OS file descriptor leak under high churn no explicit close on roll-over Log fd count metric; assert drop closes

2.7 Performance Concerns

  • BufWriter batch: amortize write syscalls; flush + fsync at commit boundary, not per record.
  • pread vs mmap: pread is simpler and avoids SIGBUS; mmap considered only for read-heavy hot segments.
  • Index memory: StreamIndex HashMap entry ~64 bytes; 10 M events ≈ 640 MiB — shard by stream prefix for large stores.
  • Segment scan on recovery: linear O(segment_size); parallelize across all-but-last segments (already sealed, CRC-verified at write time).
  • Batched fsync policy: Periodic(1ms) reduces p99 latency by 5–10× vs per-write at cost of 1 ms durability window.
  • Index file I/O: sequential append only; avoid random writes; rebuild is linear read.

2.8 Edge Cases

  • Empty segment file (crash before first write): recovery treats as zero-record segment; no error.
  • Segment file with only a partial length prefix (3 bytes): torn write; truncate to 0.
  • GlobalPosition gaps in position index: must not occur by construction; assert on index insert.
  • Read from nonexistent (stream, number): LogError::NotFound — not a panic.
  • Concurrent readers during recovery: lock Log with RwLock; readers blocked until recovery completes.
  • Segment roll-over while batch in progress: batch must land entirely in one segment; if it would overflow, roll first.
  • Backward read across segment boundary: position index walk in reverse; must cross segment files correctly.

2.9 Failure Analysis

  • Disk full on append: LogError::IoError(ENOSPC); propagate up; do not leave partial record; rollback to pre-append write_offset.
  • Index file corruption: detected on recovery by invalid record size or hash mismatch; fall back to full log scan to rebuild (slow but safe); metric esdb_log_index_rebuild_total.
  • Segment file unreadable (permissions, missing): LogError::SegmentMissing; blast radius = all events in that segment unreadable; requires operator restore from backup; alert via esdb_log_segment_open_errors_total.
  • Double-open of same log dir: second process corrupts index; use flock on a LOCK file in the log directory; second open returns LogError::DirectoryLocked.

2.10 Operational Notes

  • Metrics: esdb_log_append_bytes_total, esdb_log_append_duration_seconds{p50,p99}, esdb_log_segment_count, esdb_log_fsync_duration_seconds, esdb_log_recovery_truncated_bytes.
  • Log at INFO: segment roll-over, recovery summary (records recovered, bytes truncated).
  • Log at WARN: CRC mismatch during scan, partial record found and truncated.
  • MAX_SEGMENT_SIZE and SyncPolicy are runtime config; expose via server config file; changing SyncPolicy takes effect on next append without restart.
  • Segment files are immutable once sealed; safe to copy for backup without coordination.

Phase 3 — stream append + optimistic concurrency

3.1 Objectives

  • Implement esdb-stream crate: the per-stream state machine governing append atomicity and versioning.
  • append(stream, expected_version, events) -> AppendResult enforces ExpectedVersion atomically.
  • Assign gap-free EventNumbers starting at 0; disallow skips or reuse.
  • WrongExpectedVersion error on version mismatch; surface stream identity in error.
  • Idempotent re-append: detect duplicate EventIds; return original AppendResult without writing.
  • Soft-delete tombstone: delete_stream(stream) appends a system event; subsequent appends to a tombstoned stream return StreamDeleted. Backed by esdb-log (Phase 2) for persistence.

3.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

<!-- Theory anchors: P3 (Optimistic Concurrency on Append), I2 (gap-free EventNumber), I5 (WrongExpectedVersion atomicity), I6 (EventId dedup), B9 (Goetz CAS patterns), B2 (Vernon DDD aggregate version = stream tip), ADR-13 (per-stream RwLock + version compare) -->

esdb-stream/src/state.rs — StreamState

pub struct StreamState {
    pub stream:       StreamId,
    pub last_version: Option<EventNumber>,   // None = stream does not exist
    pub tombstoned:   bool,
    pub recent_ids:   LruCache<EventId, AppendResult>,  // idempotency window
}
  • last_version: None when stream has never been written, Some(n) after first append; recent_ids: fixed-capacity LRU (default 4096 entries) keyed by EventId, capacity configurable.
  • StreamState is in-memory; rebuilt from log on startup by scanning stream's events in order.

esdb-stream/src/engine.rs — StreamEngine

pub struct StreamEngine {
    states:  DashMap<StreamId, StreamState>,
    log:     Arc<Log>,           // esdb-log handle
    lock:    StripedMutex<64>,   // per-stream append serialization
}
  • StripedMutex<64>: 64-slot mutex array; stream assigned by hash(StreamId) % 64 — reduces contention.
  • StreamEngine::append(stream, expected, events) -> Result<AppendResult, AppendError> (lock held for the full sequence below; no other append to this stripe can interleave — the atomicity guarantee): acquire the stripe lock for stream; load StreamState (or create empty); reject on tombstone (StreamDeleted); if every EventId in events matches recent_ids, return the cached AppendResult (idempotent no-op); else validate ExpectedVersion against state.last_version (WrongExpectedVersion{expected,actual} on mismatch); assign gap-free EventNumbers from last_version.map(|n| n+1).unwrap_or(0); stamp EventRecord.number/.stream and assign GlobalPosition via Log::next_position(); encode and append the batch to Log (Phase 2) atomically — write all records, then a single fsync; update state.last_version/recent_ids; release the lock; return AppendResult{next_expected_version, commit_position}.
  • StreamEngine::delete_stream(stream, expected) -> Result<(), AppendError>: validates ExpectedVersion, appends the system event "$streamDeleted", sets tombstoned = true.

esdb-stream/src/errors.rs

Variant Fields Condition
WrongExpectedVersion { expected: ExpectedVersion, actual: Option<EventNumber> } Version mismatch
StreamDeleted { stream: StreamId } Write to tombstoned stream
IdempotentMismatch { event_id: EventId } Same EventId, different payload
EmptyAppend — Zero events in batch
BatchTooLarge { count: usize } Batch exceeds max (default 4096)

esdb-stream/src/read.rs

  • read_stream(stream, from: EventNumber, dir, limit) -> ReadResult — delegates to Log::read_stream.
  • read_stream_metadata(stream) -> StreamMetadata — returns { last_version, tombstoned, event_count }.

3.3 Deliverable

  • esdb-stream compiles; StreamEngine::append + delete_stream + read_stream all callable.
  • Integration test: concurrent appends from 8 threads on the same stream; verify final last_version equals total events minus 1; no gaps; no duplicates.
  • Idempotency test: append the same batch twice — log contains the events once, both calls return the same AppendResult. Tombstone test: delete stream then attempt append — StreamDeleted returned, no new records in log.

3.4 Acceptance Criteria

Check Target
cargo test -p esdb-stream 100% pass
Concurrent append test (8 threads × 1000 appends) final last_version == 7999; zero gaps
WrongExpectedVersion correct on version=5, expected=4 error returned; stream at version 5 unchanged
Idempotent re-append (same EventIds) recent_ids hit; log not written twice
IdempotentMismatch for same EventId + different data error returned
Tombstone enforcement StreamDeleted on any append post-delete
ExpectedVersion::NoStream on existing stream WrongExpectedVersion
ExpectedVersion::StreamExists on nonexistent stream WrongExpectedVersion

3.5 Conformance Fixtures

File Purpose Expected outcome
fixtures/stream/append_sequential_10.json 10 sequential appends, ExactVersion versions 0–9, no error
fixtures/stream/wrong_version_stale.json append with expected=3, actual=5 WrongExpectedVersion{expected:Exact(3),actual:Some(5)}
fixtures/stream/idempotent_same_batch.json same batch submitted twice single log entry; same AppendResult
fixtures/stream/idempotent_mismatch.json same EventId, different data IdempotentMismatch
fixtures/stream/no_stream_on_existing.json NoStream on stream at version 2 WrongExpectedVersion
fixtures/stream/tombstone_append.json append after delete StreamDeleted
fixtures/stream/any_version_concurrent.json 4 threads, ExpectedVersion::Any all succeed; versions gap-free
fixtures/stream/empty_batch.json zero events EmptyAppend error

3.6 Pitfalls

Pitfall Manifestation Root cause Debug signal
Lock released before log fsync, or a partial batch write Two concurrent appends assign the same EventNumber, or EventNumbers 0–3 commit while 4–9 are lost State updated before fsync confirms; log append not atomic across all records Hold the lock through fsync, never release early; use a single Log::append_batch that writes all-or-none
LRU cache eviction loses idempotency window Replay of old EventId creates duplicate Cache capacity too small for burst Monitor esdb_stream_idempotency_cache_evictions_total
ExpectedVersion::Any with gap after crash recovery Last version read from stale in-memory state State not rebuilt from log on restart Always rebuild StreamState from log on StreamEngine::open
IdempotentMismatch not checked Caller gets Ok for a semantically different event LRU stores AppendResult but not payload hash Store data_hash in recent_ids value; compare on hit
Tombstone flag lost on restart Deleted stream accepts appends after crash tombstoned field not persisted Derive tombstoned from presence of $streamDeleted system event during state rebuild

3.7 Performance Concerns

  • Stripe lock width: 64 stripes → contention ≈ 1/64 per stripe under uniform stream distribution (increase for hotspot workloads); DashMap read contention: StreamState lookup is a shared-read, so hot streams should cache last_version in a local variable inside the lock to avoid repeated map lookups.
  • Batch encoding: encode all EventRecords before acquiring the lock; the lock is held only for version check + log write + state update; Log::append_batch: single BufWriter flush + fsync for the batch, never fsync per record.
  • LRU cache allocation: pre-allocate recent_ids with capacity to avoid per-insert alloc on hot paths; AppendResult return: zero-copy, struct is 16 bytes, returned by value.

3.8 Edge Cases

  • Stream name = "$" prefix is reserved for system streams (append returns ReservedStream for user callers); EventNumber at u64::MAX - 1 overflows on increment and returns StreamFull.
  • Batch with duplicate EventIds within the same batch (not a re-submit): DuplicateEventIdInBatch, whole batch rejected; ExpectedVersion::Exact(n) where n > current version (optimistic future version): WrongExpectedVersion.
  • delete_stream with ExpectedVersion::NoStream on a nonexistent stream is an idempotent success (already absent); a concurrent second delete after the first commits gets StreamDeleted from the tombstone check.
  • Read backward from the EventNumber::MAX sentinel is treated as "start from last event"; StreamState rebuild from a log with mixed streams scans the log once and builds all states — O(total events), but only at startup.

3.9 Failure Analysis

  • Stripe lock starvation (writer holding lock due to slow fsync): other writers on same stripe blocked; observable as esdb_stream_append_lock_wait_seconds spike; remedy: SyncPolicy::Periodic to reduce fsync latency.
  • DashMap memory growth (unbounded stream count): StreamState per stream; implement eviction of cold stream states after TTL; metric esdb_stream_state_cache_size.
  • Log write failure mid-batch: LogError::IoError; StreamState must not be updated; return error; client must retry; invariant I4 preserved.
  • Recovery rebuilds wrong last_version (index corrupt): stream accepts duplicate version; detected by WrongExpectedVersion on subsequent correct writer; trigger full log re-scan via operator command.
  • Idempotency window miss (event replayed after cache eviction): downstream sees duplicate; mitigated by persistent subscription dedup (Phase 7); surface as esdb_stream_idempotency_miss_total metric.

3.10 Operational Notes

  • Metrics: esdb_stream_append_total{stream_category,result}, esdb_stream_append_duration_seconds, esdb_stream_wrong_version_total, esdb_stream_idempotency_hits_total, esdb_stream_idempotency_cache_evictions_total, esdb_stream_state_cache_size. Structured log fields on every append (TRACE): stream_id, expected_version, event_count, result_version, commit_position. Warn on WrongExpectedVersion if rate exceeds 100/s (sign of a concurrency bug in a client).
  • StreamEngine state is recoverable from log — no separate WAL needed; recovery time proportional to event count; expose esdb_stream_recovery_duration_seconds metric. Config knobs: stripe_count (default 64), idempotency_cache_capacity (default 4096), max_batch_size (default 4096 events), max_event_data_bytes (default 4 MiB).

Phase 4 — Global Log + Commit Positions

4.1 Objectives

  • Implement the $all global log in esdb-log: every committed event receives a unique monotonic GlobalPosition(u64).
  • Define commit/prepare position semantics: prepare = offset at which leader proposes; commit = position assigned after quorum (in later phases), single-node = same value.
  • Make GlobalPosition the universal cursor for subscriptions, projections, and replication.
  • Guarantee I3: positions strictly increasing, unique, assigned at commit time, never reused.
  • Expose global_log::append(entry) -> GlobalPosition and global_log::read_from(pos, limit) -> Vec<EventRecord>.

4.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

<!-- Theory anchors: P4 (Total Order in Global Log), I3 (commit positions strictly increasing), P6 (Spanner commit timestamp semantics), P5 (Kafka offset as universal cursor), ADR-23 (per-partition position space), R2 (Kafka GlobalLog/offset analogue) -->

esdb-log/src/global.rs

  • GlobalLog struct: wraps a dedicated segment set distinct from per-stream segments; owns a AtomicU64 position counter (persisted as the tail of the last committed segment).
  • Segment file naming: global-{start_position:020}.seg; each file max 256 MB (configurable via LogConfig::global_segment_bytes).
  • Record layout per entry (binary, length-prefixed):
Field Size Notes
magic 4 B 0xE5DB_4A11
length 4 B LE u32, payload only
position 8 B LE u64, assigned monotonically
term 8 B LE u64, Raft term (0 for single-node)
stream_id_len 2 B LE u16
stream_id var UTF-8
event_id 16 B UUID bytes
event_number 8 B LE u64
payload var serialized EventRecord
crc32 4 B over all preceding bytes
  • GlobalLog::append(&mut self, record: &EventRecord, term: u64) -> Result<GlobalPosition>: assign pos = fetch_add(1), write entry, fsync (or batch-fsync per LogConfig::sync_every), update in-memory tail index position -> (segment_id, byte_offset).
  • GlobalLog::read_from(&self, from: GlobalPosition, limit: usize, direction: ReadDirection) -> Result<ReadResult<EventRecord>>: forward = scan tail index from from; backward = scan index descending.
  • GlobalLog::head_position(&self) -> GlobalPosition: returns latest committed position.
  • Position index (global_index.rs): BTreeMap<GlobalPosition, (SegmentId, u64)>; rebuilt on open by scanning all global segments if absent or stale.
  • Recovery: on open, scan last segment from known good position, truncate any partial (CRC-failing) tail entry; restore counter from last valid position.
  • esdb-core update: expose GlobalPosition::ZERO, GlobalPosition::MAX, GlobalPosition::next(&self) -> GlobalPosition.
  • Observability: emit tracing::info! on every segment roll; expose metrics::gauge!("esdb.global_log.head_position") and metrics::counter!("esdb.global_log.append_total").

4.3 Deliverable

  • GlobalLog compiles, appends entries, assigns monotonic positions, fsyncs to disk, reads back in commit order.
  • Single-node integration test: append 10 000 events via esdb-stream, verify all appear in $all read with strictly increasing positions and correct EventRecord fields.
  • global_index rebuilt from segments after simulated crash (truncated last segment).

4.4 Acceptance Criteria

Check Target
Monotonicity pos[i+1] == pos[i] + 1 for every pair in a 10 000-event sequence
CRC pass rate 100% on clean write; torn-tail test recovers correctly
Recovery idempotence open+close 100x produces same head_position
Read completeness read_from(GlobalPosition::ZERO, 10000) returns all 10 000 records in order
Throughput baseline >= 50 000 appends/s single thread with fsync-per-batch (batch=100)

4.5 Conformance Fixtures

Fixture Purpose Expected outcome
fixtures/global_monotonic.bin 1 000 pre-encoded entries with known positions Read-back matches positions exactly
fixtures/global_torn_tail.bin last entry truncated mid-CRC Recovery truncates it; head_position = N-1
fixtures/global_duplicate_position.bin two entries with same position (corrupt) Open fails with CorruptGlobalLog error
fixtures/global_roundtrip.json EventRecord field values Binary encode+decode == original
proptest: global_order random N appends in parallel positions are unique, monotonic, all < N

4.6 Pitfalls

Pitfall Manifestation Debug signal Recovery
Counter not persisted After crash counter resets to 0, positions repeat Duplicate GlobalPosition in read Derive counter from last segment tail on open
Index out of sync Read misses events after restart head_position > last_indexed_position Full index rebuild pass
Torn write not detected Partial entry served CRC mismatch on random reads CRC check on every read; truncate tail on open
AtomicU64 increment before write Position assigned but entry not written Gap in positions Assign position inside segment write lock; rollback counter on write failure
Segment roll races Two threads roll at same boundary Duplicate start_position for two segment files Single-writer mutex around roll decision
Backward read off-by-one Returns one extra event next_cursor test Use exclusive lower bound in backward scan

4.7 Performance Concerns

  • Batch fsync: group up to sync_every (default 256) appends before a single fdatasync; reduces syscall overhead from O(N) to O(N/256).
  • Index as BTreeMap is cache-friendly for forward scans; avoid HashMap for ordered iteration.
  • Keep the tail index in memory; disk index written periodically (every 10 000 entries or on segment roll) to bound rebuild time.
  • Avoid copying EventRecord payload into the index; store only (SegmentId, byte_offset).
  • Pre-allocate segment files (fallocate) to avoid filesystem extent fragmentation and improve sequential write throughput.
  • GlobalPosition is a u64; reads use binary search on sorted (GlobalPosition, offset) pairs stored in a Vec for cache efficiency at small to medium scale.

4.8 Edge Cases

  • GlobalPosition overflow at u64::MAX: enforce MAX_POSITION guard; return PositionExhausted error.
  • Read with from > head_position: return empty ReadResult with is_end = true.
  • Concurrent appends from multiple streams: position counter must be atomic; segment write must be serialized.
  • Segment file deleted externally: detect missing file on open; return SegmentMissing error with recovery hint.
  • Empty $all (first node startup): head_position = GlobalPosition::ZERO, read_from returns empty.
  • Schema evolution of EventRecord inside global log: frame stores opaque payload bytes; deserialization version handled at the esdb-event layer.

4.9 Failure Analysis

Failure Detection Blast radius Recovery
Disk full during append write() returns ENOSPC Append rejected; $all intact Free space; restart; no data loss
fsync failure OS returns EIO Last batch may be un-durable Truncate torn tail on next open
Index file corruption CRC mismatch on index load Slows open (full rebuild) Rebuild from segments; no data loss
Counter file corruption Starts from 0 Position collision Rebuild from segment tail on open
Segment file corruption mid-stream CRC fail on interior record Reads fail past corrupt entry Mark segment bad; return error + alert; manual recovery or segment restore from replica

4.10 Operational Notes

  • Metric esdb.global_log.head_position — dashboard gauge; rate-of-change = write throughput.
  • Metric esdb.global_log.segment_count — alert on unbounded growth.
  • Log line on segment roll: INFO global_log: rolled segment start_position={} path={}.
  • esdb-cli global tail subcommand: stream last N events from $all by position.
  • Backup strategy: snapshot all global segment files + index; restore by copying files then opening GlobalLog.
  • No online compaction in Phase 4; scavenging addressed in Phase 9.

Phase 5 — Read API

5.1 Objectives

  • Implement the full read surface in esdb-stream and a new esdb-query façade crate.
  • Read stream forward/backward from EventNumber with count limit and paging.
  • Read $all forward/backward from GlobalPosition with paging.
  • Read by category (stream-id prefix) and by event-type (server-side filter). Paging contract: ReadResult { events: Vec<EventRecord>, next: Option<Cursor>, is_end: bool }.
  • Consistent-reads semantics: reads observe only committed events; in-flight appends are not visible. Constraint: all read paths are zero-copy where possible (return Bytes slices into mapped segments).

5.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

<!-- Theory anchors: P9 (Read Models Are Disposable — read path must not modify state), B4 (Akidau streams-as-logs + tables-as-materialized-views), P5 (Kafka offset cursor paging), R8 (Axon global index for category reads), ADR-10 (custom sorted binary index) -->

esdb-stream/src/read.rs

  • StreamReader::read(stream: &StreamId, from: EventNumber, direction: ReadDirection, count: usize) -> Result<ReadResult<EventRecord>>: resolve stream index (stream,number)->offset, load segment, deserialize, return up to count records.
  • Backward read: start from min(stream_head, from), walk index descending.
  • StreamReader::read_last(stream: &StreamId) -> Result<Option<EventRecord>>: shortcut for latest event.
  • Tombstone check: if stream is tombstoned, read returns StreamDeleted error.

esdb-query/src/lib.rs (new crate, depends on esdb-log, esdb-stream, esdb-event)

  • AllReader::read_all(from: GlobalPosition, direction: ReadDirection, count: usize, filter: Option<EventFilter>) -> Result<ReadResult<EventRecord>>
  • EventFilter enum:
EventFilter = ByCategory(String)
            | ByEventType(String)
            | ByStreamId(StreamId)
            | All
  • Category filter: stream category = prefix before first - in StreamId; applied server-side during $all scan.
  • Event-type filter: match EventRecord.event_type string during scan; no secondary index in Phase 5.
  • Paging: Cursor = enum { StreamCursor(EventNumber), GlobalCursor(GlobalPosition) }; next cursor returned is exclusive (i.e., first unseen position/number).
  • Consistent snapshot: reads take a read_epoch = global_log.head_position() at start; return only events with position <= read_epoch.
  • In-memory read cache: optional LruCache<(StreamId, EventNumber), Arc<EventRecord>> in StreamReader; bounded by CacheConfig::max_bytes.

esdb-query/src/category_index.rs

  • Optional secondary index BTreeMap<(String, GlobalPosition), ()> built lazily as $all is scanned; written to category-{name}.idx on disk.
  • Phase 5 builds it in-memory only; disk persistence in Phase 9.

API signatures (no bodies):

pub fn read_stream(stream: &StreamId, from: EventNumber, dir: ReadDirection, count: usize) -> Result<ReadResult<EventRecord>>;
pub fn read_all(from: GlobalPosition, dir: ReadDirection, count: usize, filter: Option<EventFilter>) -> Result<ReadResult<EventRecord>>;
pub fn read_by_category(category: &str, from: GlobalPosition, count: usize) -> Result<ReadResult<EventRecord>>;
pub fn read_by_event_type(event_type: &str, from: GlobalPosition, count: usize) -> Result<ReadResult<EventRecord>>;

Observability: metrics::histogram!("esdb.read.latency_us", ...) tagged by read_type; metrics::counter!("esdb.read.events_returned").

5.3 Deliverable

  • esdb-query crate compiles; all four read functions pass integration tests.
  • Category and event-type filter return correct subsets from a mixed 50 000-event dataset.
  • Paging: calling read_all repeatedly from next cursor exhausts $all without gap or duplicate.
  • Backward stream read returns events in descending EventNumber order.

5.4 Acceptance Criteria

Check Target
Forward stream read correctness All events in order, no gaps, is_end=true at stream head
Backward stream read correctness Descending EventNumber, no duplicates
$all paging completeness 100 000 events retrieved in pages of 1 000; union == full set
Category filter precision Only events from matching category returned (property test with 10 categories)
Event-type filter recall No false negatives on exact event_type match
Consistent read epoch Read started before append does not see the appended event
p99 read latency (warm cache) < 2 ms for 100-event page from stream
p99 read latency (cold, $all, no filter) < 10 ms for 1 000-event page

fixtures/read_stream_backward.json Same 500 events, backward from 499 Descending numbers, is_end=true when next=None

fixtures/read_empty_stream.json Non-existent stream events=[], is_end=true, no error
fixtures/read_tombstoned_stream.json Soft-deleted stream StreamDeleted error
fixtures/read_past_head.json from > stream_head events=[], is_end=true
proptest: paging_completeness Random N events, random page sizes All events recovered exactly once

5.6 Pitfalls

Pitfall Manifestation Debug signal Recovery
Off-by-one in next cursor Page N overlaps or skips event at boundary Missing/duplicate events in paging test Use exclusive upper bound; test with count=1
Stale read epoch Read returns in-flight uncommitted event position > head_position at read start Snapshot head_position before first fetch; filter strictly
Category string case sensitivity Order- vs order- miss Filter returns 0 Normalize category to lowercase on ingest and filter
Filter applied post-fetch Full $all scan even with tight filter CPU spike on large event sets Apply filter inside segment scan, not after deserialization
Backward $all missing events Index built forward only read_all(backward) skips entries Build backward-capable index (sorted desc view or reverse traverse)
LRU cache serving deleted stream events Cache hit after tombstone Tombstoned stream returns events Invalidate cache entry on tombstone write
Count=0 read Panic or infinite loop --- Guard: count == 0 returns empty ReadResult immediately

5.7 Performance Concerns

  • Segment mmap: use mmap for read-heavy workloads to avoid redundant read() syscalls on hot segments; LRU cache is bounded by bytes, not entry count, to avoid memory blowup on large payloads.
  • Category index: linear scan of $all is O(N) without an index, O(category_events) with one (built lazily); page size tuning defaults to 4 096 events per read — larger pages amortize syscall overhead but increase latency tail.
  • Zero-copy Bytes: return Bytes::copy_from_slice from the mmap region, avoiding heap allocation per event; avoid String::clone per event in the filter path, comparing &str directly against segment-resident data.
  • Parallel category scans: rayon::join for multi-category reads; single-category is sequential.

5.8 Edge Cases

  • from = GlobalPosition::MAX backward returns the single event at head, or empty if the store is empty; a stream with 1 event returns it on both forward and backward reads (next = None, is_end = true).
  • Count overflow (usize::MAX as limit) returns all, capped internally at MAX_PAGE_SIZE = 65536; $all read during a concurrent append storm is protected by the read epoch, which prevents partial batches from appearing mid-page.
  • Filter by an unknown category/event-type returns an empty ReadResult, not an error; a schema-evolved event mid-stream delegates deserialization to esdb-event, preserving unknown fields as opaque bytes (forward compatibility).
  • Segment file rolled mid-read: GlobalLog keeps open File handles for in-use segments; a roll only creates a new segment, it never truncates the existing one.

5.9 Failure Analysis

Failure Detection Blast radius Recovery
Segment file missing during read open() returns ENOENT Reads for that position range fail Return SegmentMissing error; alert operator; restore from replica or backup
CRC mismatch on read CRC check during deserialization Single event unreadable Return CorruptRecord error; skip or halt depending on mode
Index gap (event in log but not indexed) Event missing from read result Silent data loss on read Periodic consistency check: walk log, verify index coverage
OOM during large page read Allocator OOM Node crash Enforce MAX_PAGE_SIZE; use streaming read for large ranges
Hot read path lock contention p99 latency spike Read throughput degrades Switch to RwLock for index; prefer lock-free atomic snapshot for head_position

5.10 Operational Notes

  • Metrics: esdb.read.events_returned by read_type (stream/all/category/event_type) for the throughput dashboard; esdb.read.latency_us p50/p99/p999, alert if p99 > 5 ms on stream reads; esdb.read.cache_hit_ratio, alert if < 20% on hot workloads (cache misconfigured).
  • Log slow reads (> 50 ms) at WARN with stream_id, from, count, elapsed_ms; esdb-cli read stream <id> --from 0 --count 100 uses this API.
  • Backward read on a tombstoned stream always returns StreamDeleted, never partially serves events; category index is in-memory in Phase 5, Phase 9 persists it with no API change (transparent upgrade).

Phase 6 — Snapshots + Aggregate Load

6.1 Objectives

  • Implement esdb-snapshot: a separate snapshot store keyed by (StreamId, EventNumber), physically isolated from the event log (dedicated snapshots/ directory). Write Snapshot { stream, version: EventNumber, state: Bytes, position: GlobalPosition } periodically from the aggregate layer.
  • Load aggregate = newest snapshot with version <= target + replay tail events from version+1 onward, bounded by a configurable max tail (SnapshotConfig::max_tail_events, default 10 000).
  • Snapshot invalidation on schema change via a schema_version: u32 field in Snapshot. Uphold I8 (projection determinism) and I9 (snapshot fidelity).

6.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

<!-- Theory anchors: I9 (snapshot fidelity = snap+tail == full replay), P11 (Chandy-Lamport consistent snapshot), B3 (Zimarev snapshot loading algorithm), ADR-14 (snapshot store isolation), P2 (Log Is Source of Truth — snapshot is a cache, not normative) -->

esdb-snapshot/src/lib.rs

  • SnapshotStore: opens snapshots/<stream_id_hash>/ directory; one file per snapshot.
  • Snapshot file name: {stream_id_hash}-{version:020}-{schema_version:08}.snap
  • Binary format:
Field Size Notes
magic 4 B 0xE5DB_5A70
schema_version 4 B LE u32
stream_id_len 2 B LE u16
stream_id var UTF-8
version 8 B LE u64 (EventNumber)
position 8 B LE u64 (GlobalPosition)
state_len 4 B LE u32
state var opaque Bytes (caller-controlled encoding)
crc32 4 B over all preceding bytes
  • SnapshotStore::write(snap: &Snapshot) -> Result<()>: write atomically (write to .tmp, fsync, rename).
  • SnapshotStore::latest(stream: &StreamId, max_version: EventNumber, schema_version: u32) -> Result<Option<Snapshot>>: scan directory listing, filter by schema_version match and version <= max_version, return highest matching version.
  • SnapshotStore::prune(stream: &StreamId, keep: usize) -> Result<()>: delete older snapshots beyond keep count per stream to bound disk usage.
  • SnapshotStore::delete(stream: &StreamId, version: EventNumber) -> Result<()>: explicit invalidation.

esdb-snapshot/src/loader.rs

  • AggregateLoader::load<S: Aggregate>(store: &SnapshotStore, reader: &StreamReader, stream: &StreamId, target: EventNumber, schema_version: u32) -> Result<S>: calls store.latest(stream, target, schema_version); if Some(snap), deserializes snap.state into S and sets replay start to snap.version + 1, else starts from EventNumber(0) with empty initial state; reads the tail via reader.read(stream, replay_start, Forward, max_tail_events) and folds events into state via S::apply(state, event); if is_end == false after max_tail_events, returns TailTooLong (snapshot stale/missing for a large stream); otherwise returns the final state.

Aggregate trait sketch (signatures only):

pub trait Aggregate: Sized {
    fn initial() -> Self;
    fn apply(self, event: &EventRecord) -> Self;
    fn schema_version() -> u32;
}

esdb-snapshot/src/policy.rs

  • SnapshotPolicy enum: EveryN(u64) | EveryDuration(Duration) | Manual.
  • SnapshotPolicy::should_snapshot(last_snap_version: EventNumber, current_version: EventNumber, elapsed: Duration) -> bool.

Observability: metrics::histogram!("esdb.snapshot.tail_events_replayed"), metrics::counter!("esdb.snapshot.writes_total"), metrics::counter!("esdb.snapshot.loads_total").

6.3 Deliverable

  • esdb-snapshot crate compiles; AggregateLoader::load reconstructs correct aggregate state.
  • Integration test: append 100 000 events to a stream, write snapshot at 50 000, load from snapshot + tail in < 200 ms.
  • Snapshot invalidation test: schema_version mismatch causes full replay from event 0.
  • TailTooLong returned when snapshot absent and stream exceeds max_tail_events.

6.4 Acceptance Criteria

Check Target
Snapshot fidelity (I9) load_via_snapshot(stream, v) state == full_replay(stream, v) state for all v in proptest
Schema invalidation schema_version mismatch → snapshot ignored → full replay from 0
TailTooLong guard Stream of 20 000 events, max_tail_events=10000, no snapshot → TailTooLong error
Atomic write Kill process mid-write → no corrupt snapshot on restart (.tmp leftover cleaned up)
Prune correctness After prune(keep=3) exactly 3 snapshots remain per stream
Tail replay bound 10 000-event tail replayed in < 100 ms on reference hardware
State identity Aggregate::apply is deterministic: same events same order always same state

6.5 Conformance Fixtures

Fixture Purpose Expected outcome
fixtures/snapshot_load_basic.json Snapshot at v=500, tail 200 events Final state matches full replay to v=699
fixtures/snapshot_schema_mismatch.snap schema_version differs from current Ignored; full replay used
fixtures/snapshot_corrupt_crc.snap CRC intentionally wrong CorruptSnapshot error; fallback to replay
fixtures/snapshot_no_snapshot.json Stream of 5 000 events, no snapshot Full replay from 0; state correct
fixtures/snapshot_prune.json 10 snapshots present, keep=3 7 deleted; oldest retained is #8
proptest: snapshot_fidelity Random streams, random snapshot versions snap+tail == full_replay for all configs
fixtures/snapshot_tmp_leftover.snap.tmp Leftover tmp file from crashed write Cleaned on SnapshotStore::open; no error

6.6 Pitfalls

Pitfall Manifestation Debug signal Recovery
Non-atomic snapshot write Corrupt snapshot on crash during write CRC mismatch on open Always write to .tmp, fsync, rename; clean .tmp on open
Schema version not embedded Old snapshot used after aggregate evolution State deserialization error or wrong state Include schema_version in filename and header; check before use
Tail too long without guard load hangs replaying millions of events tail_events_replayed metric spikes Enforce max_tail_events; alert if exceeded
Snapshot version off-by-one Replays one event twice or skips first State diverges from full replay Replay starts at snap.version + 1, not snap.version
Snapshot directory per stream grows unbounded Disk exhaustion snapshot_count_per_stream metric Auto-prune on write: keep last N
Concurrent writers to same stream Two processes write conflicting snapshots Non-deterministic latest selection Use stream-scoped file lock or single-writer invariant
Stale snapshot after scavenge Snapshot references events that have been scavenged Load fails to find tail events On scavenge, invalidate snapshots whose version is below scavenge watermark

6.7 Performance Concerns

  • Snapshot file size: state is caller-opaque bytes; large states (> 10 MB) slow write and load — recommend caller-side compression (zstd). Directory listing per latest() call: cache the latest snapshot version in memory per stream to avoid repeated readdir.
  • read() tail events: prefetch in batches of 4 096 to amortize segment seek cost; Aggregate::apply is a hot path during replay, so avoid heap allocation per event and use Bytes::slice for payload access.
  • Prune is a background operation that must not block the load path; snapshot write fsync is a single fsync after rename (not after the .tmp write) — the rename is crash-safe on Linux ext4/xfs with dir_sync.

6.8 Edge Cases

  • Target EventNumber(0): load initial state, no events to replay, snapshot at v=0 valid; stream does not exist: latest() returns None, reader.read() returns empty, Aggregate::initial() returned.
  • Snapshot version > stream head (e.g., replicated partial data): skip and use next-lower or full replay; state = empty bytes is valid for stateless aggregates (counter at zero) and must not error.
  • Multiple schema versions coexist on disk: latest() filters strictly by schema_version, older snapshots ignored; snapshot written at version = EventNumber::MAX has no possible tail, load returns state directly.
  • Crash between snapshot write and event append: snapshot at v=N exists but stream head is v=N-1; load detects a zero-event tail and returns the snapshot state directly (correct by I9).

6.9 Failure Analysis

Failure Detection Blast radius Recovery
All snapshots corrupt for a stream CRC fail on every .snap load Aggregate load falls back to full replay Full replay from 0; if max_tail_events exceeded → operator must raise limit or restore backup
Snapshot directory inaccessible open() EPERM/ENOENT All aggregate loads degrade to full replay Fix filesystem permissions; restart
Disk full during snapshot write write() ENOSPC .tmp leftover; no corrupt snapshot Clean .tmp; free space; retry
Tail events missing (scavenged) read() returns fewer events than expected for tail tail_events_replayed < expected Restore from backup or replica; scavenge policy must preserve events referenced by live snapshots
TailTooLong in production Operator alert; aggregate not loadable Single aggregate unavailable Write snapshot at current head; retry load

6.10 Operational Notes

  • Metric esdb.snapshot.tail_events_replayed histogram: p99 alert if > 5 000 (snapshot policy too infrequent); metric esdb.snapshot.age_events per stream (stream_head - latest_snapshot_version): alert if > max_tail_events * 0.8.
  • Log on snapshot write: INFO snapshot: wrote stream={} version={} schema={} bytes={}; log on schema invalidation: WARN snapshot: ignoring snapshot schema={} expected={} stream={}.
  • esdb-cli snapshot write <stream> <version> triggers a manual snapshot; esdb-cli snapshot list <stream> shows available snapshots, schema versions, sizes.
  • Deployment note: when schema_version increments, old snapshots are automatically bypassed; the warm-up period may cause elevated replay latency, so schedule snapshot writes before deploying schema-breaking changes.
  • Backup: include snapshots/ alongside event log segments; a snapshot without its corresponding tail events is unusable.

Phase 7 — Subscriptions (Catch-Up + Live)

7.1 Objectives

  • Implement esdb-subscribe crate: catch-up from any GlobalPosition, seamless transition to live delivery, no gap or duplicate at the boundary. Two subscription kinds: Volatile (in-memory, lost on restart) and Persistent (checkpointed, resumes across restarts). Filter dimensions: single stream by StreamId, stream category (prefix match), event-type, or $all.
  • Backpressure: a slow consumer must not block the log or other subscriptions — bounded channel with configurable capacity, apply/park semantics. Produce Subscription and Checkpoint types; integrate with esdb-log's global position cursor and esdb-stream's per-stream reader.

7.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

<!-- Theory anchors: I3 (commit positions strictly increasing — cursor for catch-up), P5 (Idempotent Checkpointed Projections — at-least-once + idempotency), B1 (Kleppmann Ch.11 exactly-once via consumer offsets), P14 (Zaharia contrast: micro-batch vs continuous push), R1 (EventStoreDB catch-up/live boundary), ADR-03 (Tokio bounded channel backpressure) -->

Module layout (esdb-subscribe/src/):

File Responsibility
lib.rs public API: SubscriptionManager, SubscribeRequest, SubscriptionHandle
catch_up.rs batch-reads $all from GlobalPosition; drives esdb-log sequential scan
live.rs receives EventRecord notifications from esdb-log post-commit hook
boundary.rs catches the catch-up / live transition: deduplicates overlap window
filter.rs SubscriptionFilter enum + matches(record) predicate
checkpoint.rs durable Checkpoint store; fsync-on-update for persistent subs
backpressure.rs BoundedSender<EventRecord>; park() and nack() routing
persistent.rs PersistentSubscription: group, competing consumers, ack tracking

Catch-up loop: open a read cursor at from_position in the esdb-log global index; read batches (default 256 records), apply filter, send to the consumer channel, and track the highest position read (catch_up_head); when the log reports no more records, atomically begin collecting live notifications into a transition_buffer while finishing the last batch, then drain transition_buffer deduplicating by GlobalPosition (skip any position already sent) and switch to the live-delivery path, discarding transition_buffer.

Live delivery:

  • esdb-log exposes post_commit_notify(tx: Sender<GlobalPosition>) hook (registered at startup).
  • On notification: fetch record by position, apply filter, send to subscriber channel.
  • Multiple subscribers share one notify channel; fan-out via per-subscription tasks.

Volatile subscription: Subscription { kind: Volatile } — channel dropped on handle drop; no Checkpoint written.

Persistent subscription: Checkpoint { subscription_or_projection, position } is written to checkpoint.dat (append-only log of checkpoints, keyed by sub ID); resume reads the latest checkpoint position and starts catch-up from there. Competing consumers: PersistentSubscription holds a VecDeque<PendingAck> per consumer with round-robin or work-stealing dispatch; ack advances the checkpoint, nack re-enqueues, park moves to the dead-letter queue after max_retry.

Filter implementation:

SubscriptionFilter::Stream(StreamId)  → record.stream == id
SubscriptionFilter::Category(prefix)  → record.stream.category() == prefix
SubscriptionFilter::EventType(et)     → record.event_type == et
SubscriptionFilter::All               → true

Backpressure: tokio::sync::mpsc bounded channel (capacity configurable, default 1024). If channel full, send returns Err(Full) → subscriber task applies backpressure: pause catch-up reads, yield; for persistent subs, record SendSlowConsumer metric.

Observability hooks: sub.lag_events: u64 = global head position − subscription's current checkpoint position; sub.lag_bytes: u64 derived from index size estimate; emit subscription_lag_position gauge per subscription ID.

7.3 Deliverable

  • SubscriptionManager::subscribe(req) -> SubscriptionHandle compiles and passes integration tests; catch-up replays all matching historical events then delivers live events without gap or duplicate.
  • Persistent subscriptions survive process restart and resume from the last checkpoint; competing-consumer round-robin distributes across N consumers with ack/nack/park all functioning; saturating a consumer channel pauses catch-up reads without blocking other subscriptions.

7.4 Acceptance Criteria

Test Requirement
test_catchup_no_gap append 10k events, subscribe from 0, receive all 10k in order
test_live_no_duplicate append during transition; zero duplicate GlobalPositions received
test_persistent_resume kill subscriber at position P, restart, resume from P (no re-delivery before P)
test_category_filter 1000 events across 10 categories; subscription on one category sees exactly its slice
test_backpressure_no_deadlock fill consumer channel; other subscriptions unaffected; system unblocked after drain
test_competing_consumers 3 consumers on group; each event delivered exactly once across group
test_nack_retry nack event N times; after max_retry parks to dead-letter; no infinite loop
Lag metric subscription_lag_position gauge decreases as consumer catches up; reaches 0 when live

7.5 Conformance Fixtures

Fixture file Purpose
fixtures/sub_catchup_100.bin 100-event log; expected subscription output (in-order, position-stamped)
fixtures/sub_transition_overlap.bin events appended during transition window; expected deduplicated output
fixtures/sub_persistent_checkpoint.dat pre-written checkpoint at position 500; verify resume starts at 500
fixtures/sub_category_events.bin mixed-category log; expected per-category filtered slices
fixtures/sub_nack_deadletter.bin nack sequence; expected dead-letter queue contents after max_retry
fixtures/sub_backpressure_order.bin high-rate log; verify order preserved despite backpressure pauses

Malformed: truncated checkpoint file → reset to position 0; corrupt record mid-catch-up → skip + emit corrupt_record_skipped metric.

7.6 Pitfalls

Pitfall Manifestation Root Cause Detection / Recovery
Gap or duplicate at catch-up/live boundary missed events between last batch and first live notification, or an event delivered twice race: notification arrives before batch finishes; overlap window not deduplicated transition_buffer overlap window; dedup by GlobalPosition in the boundary module
Checkpoint overwrite without fsync resume from wrong position after crash OS buffer not flushed fsync checkpoint file after every write
Slow consumer blocks fast consumer all subscriptions stall shared channel or mutex per-subscription independent bounded channels
Competing consumer starvation one consumer receives all events unfair dispatch round-robin or work-stealing via VecDeque rotation
Nack storm CPU spike, retry loop uncapped retries on poisoned event exponential backoff + max_retry + dead-letter park
Filter applied after read wasted I/O for unmatched events filtering after fetch from log push filter into log cursor when index supports it

7.7 Performance Concerns

  • Catch-up batch size: 256 records balances memory (≤1 MB at 4 KB/event) vs syscall frequency (exposed as config); channel allocation is per-subscription at subscribe time, avoiding shared ring-buffer contention.
  • Fan-out: single post_commit_notify sender → N subscription tasks, N atomic wake-ups, cost O(N) per commit; cap at 1024 concurrent subscribers before introducing a broadcast bus. Filter hot path: SubscriptionFilter::All is a no-op check, Category is a string-prefix check — cache category() on StreamId to avoid repeated allocation.
  • Checkpoint write amortizes fsync, batching flushes at 10 ms or every 1000 events, whichever first; live-path allocation uses a zero-copy Bytes clone for fan-out — the payload is reference-counted, not copied per subscriber.

7.8 Edge Cases

  • from_position beyond current global head immediately enters live mode with no catch-up reads; an empty stream filter (no events match) leaves the subscription open, receiving nothing until a matching event arrives.
  • Log truncated (Raft rollback, Phase 10) while catch-up is in progress: detect the position discontinuity and restart catch-up from the last good checkpoint.
  • Consumer drops its handle mid-catch-up: task detects the closed channel, shuts down cleanly, and (for a persistent sub) flushes the checkpoint at the current position; two persistent subscriptions with the same ID started concurrently: second registration returns DuplicateSubscriptionId, no double-delivery.
  • Clock skew between nodes (Phase 11) is a non-issue since subscriptions cursor on GlobalPosition (monotonic, not wall-clock); max_retry = 0 is a valid configuration meaning immediate park on first nack.

7.9 Failure Analysis

Failure Detection Blast Radius Recovery
Checkpoint file corrupted CRC mismatch on read one subscription re-delivers from 0 truncate to last valid entry; metric checkpoint_corruption_total
Log segment deleted during catch-up SegmentNotFound error from esdb-log subscription aborted re-subscribe from earliest available position; alert operator
Subscriber task panic tokio task join error one subscription lost SubscriptionManager restarts task; persistent sub resumes from checkpoint
Network drop on gRPC stream (Phase 13) gRPC stream EOF client loses live events client reconnects; catch-up from last acked position
Dead-letter queue full park() returns Err(DLQFull) events discarded alert; operator must drain DLQ before resuming

7.10 Operational Notes

  • Metrics: subscription_lag_position{id}, subscription_lag_events{id}, subscription_catchup_read_total, subscription_live_delivered_total, subscription_nack_total, subscription_dlq_depth{id}.
  • Traces: span per catch-up batch (sub.catchup.batch), span per live deliver (sub.live.deliver).
  • Logs: INFO on subscribe/unsubscribe; WARN on nack; ERROR on DLQ overflow, checkpoint corruption.
  • Deployment: persistent checkpoint files live in <data_dir>/subscriptions/<sub_id>/checkpoint.dat; back up before scavenge (Phase 9) since positions may be rewritten.
  • Rollback: persistent subscriptions are forward-only; downgrade must preserve checkpoint file format or risk re-delivery from 0.
  • Operator inspection: esdb-cli subscribe list shows all active subscriptions, lag, kind; esdb-cli subscribe info <id> shows checkpoint position and DLQ depth.

Phase 8 — Projection Engine (Read Models)

8.1 Objectives

  • Implement esdb-projection crate: register a named projection over $all or a category; an idempotent handler folds EventRecord into mutable read-model state, checkpointing position durably. At-least-once delivery from esdb-subscribe is made exactly-once-effect via handler idempotency (pure w.r.t. duplicate positions) + durable checkpoint.
  • Two modes: Continuous (runs perpetually, stays live) and OneTime (runs to current head then stops). Rebuild: reset checkpoint to 0, re-register with the Phase 7 catch-up subscription → identical final state (I8). No implementation source code in projection handlers (user-supplied closures) — the engine only manages lifecycle, state storage, and checkpointing.

8.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

<!-- Theory anchors: P5 (Idempotent Checkpointed Projections), I8 (deterministic replay = exactly-once effect), B4 (streams→tables: projection as materialized view), B3 (Zimarev projection rebuild = reset checkpoint + replay), P9 (Read Models Are Disposable), ADR-15 (pluggable projection state store) -->

Module layout (esdb-projection/src/):

File Responsibility
lib.rs ProjectionEngine, ProjectionDef, ProjectionHandle
runner.rs per-projection async task: receive events, call handler, persist state + checkpoint
state_store.rs StateStore trait + RocksDbStateStore / InMemoryStateStore impls
checkpoint_store.rs durable Checkpoint store; same format as esdb-subscribe checkpoints
handler.rs Handler trait: fn apply(&mut self, record: &EventRecord) -> Result<()>
registry.rs ProjectionRegistry: name → ProjectionDef map; enforces unique names
rebuild.rs / query.rs rebuild resets checkpoint, re-subscribes from position 0, drains to live head, marks complete; query reads current projection state (optional snapshot at a position)

Projection lifecycle state machine:

Registered → Running(CatchUp) → Running(Live) → (Continuous: stays Live | OneTime: Stopped)
         ↑                                      ↓
         └────────────── Rebuild ←─────────────┘

ProjectionDef fields:

name: String
filter: SubscriptionFilter
handler: Box<dyn Handler + Send>
mode: ProjectionMode  // Continuous | OneTime
initial_state: Bytes  // serialized initial read-model (e.g. empty JSON map)
