Runner task algorithm: read the latest Checkpoint for the projection name from checkpoint_store; subscribe via SubscriptionManager::subscribe from that position (catch-up + live, filtered); for each received EventRecord, load current state from state_store (key = projection name), call handler.apply(record, &mut state), then write the updated state and Checkpoint{position: record.position} together as a single atomic transaction via write_state_and_checkpoint(name, state, pos) (a single RocksDB WriteBatch, never state-then-checkpoint as two separate writes). For OneTime mode, detect is_end = true on the subscription read result and stop the runner, marking the projection Stopped. Rebuild calls rebuild::reset(name), which writes a checkpoint at position 0 and restarts the runner from the top.

State store: primary is an embedded key-value store (RocksDB or sled; key = projection name, value = opaque Bytes), with InMemoryStateStore for unit tests.

Idempotency contract: the engine delivers each (position, record) to the handler exactly once per checkpoint epoch; on crash + restart, catch-up resumes from the last durable checkpoint, and the atomic write_state_and_checkpoint prevents the handler from seeing an event whose state was written but whose checkpoint was not yet flushed. Handlers must still be idempotent (I8) because the subscription layer guarantees only at-least-once — if the atomic write itself fails mid-write, replay will re-deliver that position.

Observability: projection_lag_position{name} = global head − checkpoint position; projection_events_processed_total{name}, projection_handler_error_total{name}, and projection_rebuild_total{name} counters.

8.3 Deliverable

  • ProjectionEngine::register(def) -> ProjectionHandle runs catch-up then stays live for Continuous projections; state is queryable via ProjectionEngine::read_state(name) -> Bytes at any time.
  • ProjectionEngine::rebuild(name) resets checkpoint, re-replays, and yields an identical final state; OneTime projections stop at head, mark Stopped, and return the final state.
  • Checkpoint survives crash; projection resumes from the last checkpointed position.

8.4 Acceptance Criteria

Test Requirement
test_projection_catchup register projection over 50k events; final state matches expected aggregate
test_projection_idempotent_replay replay same events twice (via rebuild); identical state (I8)
test_projection_crash_resume kill runner at event 25k; restart; final state == full replay state
test_projection_onetime OneTime projection stops at head; no further events delivered post-stop
test_projection_rebuild rebuild clears state, replays from 0, converges to same final state
test_projection_category_filter category-filtered projection sees only matching streams
test_projection_lag_metric projection_lag_position gauge decreases to 0 as projection catches up
test_write_state_and_checkpoint_atomic inject crash between state write and checkpoint write; on restart state and checkpoint consistent

8.5 Conformance Fixtures

Fixture Purpose
fixtures/proj_events_1k.bin 1000-event log; expected final state JSON for a counter projection
fixtures/proj_checkpoint_mid.dat checkpoint at position 500 of 1000; verify resume produces same final state
fixtures/proj_rebuild_state.json expected state after rebuild == full-replay state
fixtures/proj_category_slice.bin mixed-category log; expected state for category-filtered projection
fixtures/proj_crash_atomic.dat state written, checkpoint not written; verify restart produces correct state

Malformed: handler.apply returns Err → log error, pause projection, emit projection_handler_error_total; do not advance checkpoint; operator must fix handler + rebuild.

8.6 Pitfalls

Pitfall Manifestation Root Cause Detection / Recovery
Non-atomic state + checkpoint diverged state after crash state file updated, checkpoint not yet written use write_state_and_checkpoint single batch; verify in crash-injection test
Non-idempotent handler, or schema drift in state duplicate counts after replay, or invalid state on apply after upgrade handler lacks replay guard; state Bytes format changed enforce EventId dedup or document the requirement (test_projection_idempotent_replay); version the state format and require rebuild on schema change
Rebuild data race two runners on same projection concurrent rebuild + running registry holds mutex; rebuild transitions state to Rebuilding before re-subscribe
Projection blocks live delivery all projections stall shared deliver channel each projection has independent subscription and channel (from Phase 7)
State store disk full WriteBatch fails large read models accumulate monitor state_store_size_bytes; alert at 80% capacity
OneTime projection never stops stays live indefinitely is_end not propagated from Phase 7 verify ReadResult.is_end propagated through subscription to runner

8.7 Performance Concerns

  • State store write per event: RocksDB WriteBatch overhead ~5–20 µs; batching 10–100 events per batch reduces write amplification at the cost of checkpoint granularity (tolerable for most read models; configurable). Handler CPU is user-supplied and the engine cannot optimize it; instrument handler_duration_us histogram per projection.
  • Fan-out: N projections each hold an independent subscription, O(N) live deliveries per commit; cap concurrent projections or use a shared $all broadcast bus for >100 projections. State read: read_state(name) is a RocksDB point lookup, O(1) but involving decompression for large states — cache in Arc<RwLock<Bytes>> if queried frequently.
  • Rebuild throughput is constrained by catch-up read rate (Phase 7) and handler throughput; parallelize multiple rebuilds only if state stores are independent. Checkpoint batch flush: every 1000 events or 100 ms, reducing fsync rate from N/event to N/batch.

8.8 Edge Cases

  • Projection registered with a filter matching zero events stays in Running(Live) indefinitely with state = initial state (valid); a handler panic is caught by tokio's catch_unwind, transitioning the projection to Faulted — operator must fix and rebuild.
  • GlobalPosition wrapping at u64::MAX is impossible at any realistic event rate (documented as a non-issue; monotonicity asserted in the append path); two projections registered with the same name get DuplicateProjectionName (registry enforces uniqueness).
  • OneTime mode against an empty log immediately reaches Stopped with initial state, no error; state store deleted externally while running returns KeyNotFound from read_state, the runner initializes from initial_state, and may produce a wrong aggregate if the checkpoint is ahead of state — detected via a state hash at checkpoint, and alerted.
  • Subscription catch-up restarts (Phase 7 log truncation): the projection runner detects the position regression and triggers rebuild automatically.

8.9 Failure Analysis

Failure Detection Blast Radius Recovery
State store corruption, or runner task OOM RocksDB checksum error, or task killed by OS one projection faulted / offline mark Faulted, rebuild from position 0; reduce state size and checkpoint more frequently, then rebuild
Handler error storm projection_handler_error_total spikes projection stuck, lag grows pause projection; operator deploys fix; rebuild
Checkpoint store full write returns DiskFull all projections halt alert; free disk; projections auto-resume; may re-deliver
Stale read of state query returns state before last event no read barrier add read_at_position(P) API that waits for checkpoint >= P

8.10 Operational Notes

  • Metrics: projection_lag_position{name}, projection_events_processed_total{name}, projection_handler_duration_us{name} histogram, projection_rebuild_total{name}, projection_faulted{name} gauge. Traces: span per projection event (proj.apply), span per rebuild (proj.rebuild). Logs: INFO on register/rebuild/stop; WARN on handler error; ERROR on fault, state store failure.
  • State files: <data_dir>/projections/<name>/state.db (RocksDB), <data_dir>/projections/<name>/checkpoint.dat. Rebuild cost is proportional to total events in log and handler throughput; estimate before triggering in production.
  • esdb-cli project list shows name, mode, checkpoint position, lag, status; esdb-cli project rebuild <name> triggers rebuild. Compatibility: state format is versioned in the initial_state header byte — handler must increment the version on schema change and handle migration or fail fast requesting rebuild.

Phase 9 — Persistence Hardening + Scavenging

9.1 Objectives

  • Harden esdb-storage + esdb-log: crash recovery via torn-write truncation and index rebuild from log; checksum verification on every read.
  • Soft-delete streams (tombstone marker in log); scavenge (compaction) removes events from deleted, expired, or maxcount-exceeded streams.
  • Scavenge rewrites segment files, preserving EventNumber and GlobalPosition semantics for all surviving events (I10).
  • Online scavenge: runs without stopping appends or reads; uses generation markers to keep current readers on old segments until swap.
  • Deliver ScavengePlan introspection: operator can preview which events will be removed before committing.

9.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

<!-- Theory anchors: P8 (Crash-Only Recovery), I4 (all-or-nothing append), I10 (scavenge preserves live event numbers/positions), P3 (Gray & Reuter WAL force-before-commit), R7 (RocksDB recovery scan), ADR-30 (RoaringBitmap scavenge), S6 (CRC-32C), S7 (fdatasync before ack) -->

Crash recovery (esdb-log/src/recovery.rs): on open, scan the last segment from the last known-good offset; per record verify CRC-32C, and on mismatch (torn write) truncate the file at that offset (ftruncate); rebuild the in-memory per-stream index by scanning all segments in order, emitting (stream, number) → offset and position → offset; write the rebuilt index to index.dat + fsync, mark the log RecoveryComplete; check invariants (per-stream numbers gap-free I2, positions strictly increasing I3) — any violation is log corruption → abort with CorruptLog.

Checksum verification (esdb-log/src/segment.rs): every record frame is [u32 length][CRC-32C over length+payload][payload bytes]; read path computes CRC on read, mismatch → ChecksumMismatch(offset, segment_id); write path computes CRC before write_all + fsync.

Soft delete (esdb-stream/src/tombstone.rs): delete_stream(stream_id) appends a Tombstone record to the per-stream log (event_type = $streamDeleted, EventNumber = next in stream) — a real event record carrying GlobalPosition, readable by $all subscribers; after tombstone, both append(stream_id, ...) and read(stream_id, ...) return StreamDeleted.

Stream expiry and maxcount (esdb-storage/src/policy.rs): per-stream RetentionPolicy { max_count: Option<u64>, max_age: Option<Duration> } stored in stream metadata; ScavengePlanner::plan(stream_id) evaluates events below the max_count cutoff and events older than max_age, marking them for removal (deleted streams mark all events for removal).

ScavengePlan schema: ScavengePlan { stream_id: Option<StreamId>, segments: Vec<SegmentPlan> }; SegmentPlan { segment_id: u64, live_ranges: Vec<(u64, u64)>, bytes_removed: u64, events_removed: u64 }, where live_ranges are (start_offset, end_offset) byte spans to keep.

Online scavenge execution (esdb-storage/src/scavenge.rs): acquire ScavengeLock (one scavenge at a time); per segment in the plan — write a new segment file <id>.seg.new containing only live-range bytes (copy, never modify in place), rewrite position→offset and stream→offset index entries for surviving events, fsync the new segment + new index, then atomically rename <id>.seg.new → <id>.seg (and the index) so new reads see the compacted version while in-flight reads on the old segment complete via file-descriptor reference; update ScavengeManifest with completed segments + bytes removed and fsync it; release ScavengeLock.

Index rebuild after scavenge: re-scan scavenged segments, update the in-memory index to drop removed offsets, emit scavenge_events_removed_total. Recovery integration tests: inject a crash at each step of scavenge; verify invariants on reopen (I10).

9.3 Deliverable

  • A crashed node recovers cleanly: torn tail truncated, index rebuilt, all committed events readable.
  • Every read verifies CRC; corrupted segment returns ChecksumMismatch not silent bad data.
  • delete_stream appends tombstone; subsequent appends/reads return StreamDeleted.
  • ScavengePlanner::plan returns inspectable ScavengePlan before any mutation.
  • Executed scavenge removes targeted events; all surviving events readable at original numbers and positions.
  • Online scavenge does not block concurrent appends or reads.

9.4 Acceptance Criteria

Test Requirement
test_crash_recovery_torn_write write N events, kill at byte K of record N+1; reopen → exactly N events, index consistent
test_crc_mismatch_detected flip one bit in segment payload; read returns ChecksumMismatch
test_index_rebuild delete index file; reopen → index rebuilt; all reads correct
test_soft_delete_tombstone delete stream; append returns StreamDeleted; $all still shows tombstone
test_scavenge_plan_preview plan on 3-stream log with deleted stream; plan lists correct segments and byte ranges
test_scavenge_preserves_live scavenge deletes stream A; events in stream B unchanged (numbers, positions, data)
test_online_scavenge_no_block concurrent append throughput ≥ 90% of baseline during scavenge
test_scavenge_crash_mid kill during scavenge step 2b; reopen; no data loss, no partial segment

9.5 Conformance Fixtures

Fixture Purpose
fixtures/seg_torn_write.bin segment with torn tail at byte 4096; expected truncation point
fixtures/seg_crc_flip.bin segment with one flipped bit; expected ChecksumMismatch error
fixtures/seg_no_index.dir segment dir with index deleted; expected rebuilt index content
fixtures/scavenge_input.dir 3 streams, stream A deleted; expected ScavengePlan JSON
fixtures/scavenge_output.dir expected post-scavenge segments (stream A absent, B+C intact with original positions)
fixtures/tombstone_event.bin expected $streamDeleted event record encoding
fixtures/retention_maxcount.dir stream with maxcount=100, 200 events; expected plan removes first 100

9.6 Pitfalls

Pitfall Manifestation Root Cause Detection / Recovery
Scavenge without atomic rename readers see partial segment in-place rewrite always write to <id>.seg.new, then rename; POSIX rename is atomic
Index not rebuilt after scavenge stale offsets point to removed events index not updated scavenge_execution always calls index_rebuild after each segment swap
CRC computed over wrong range silent corruption passes check CRC covers only payload, not length prefix CRC-32C covers [length_bytes ++ payload]; unit-tested with golden vectors
Torn write at segment boundary wrong truncation point recovery scans only last segment on open, scan last segment AND verify last record of second-to-last segment
Subscription cursors invalidated by scavenge subscription tries to read removed offset position→offset index entry deleted scavenge preserves GlobalPosition for live events; removed positions return EventScavenged error
Scavenge removes event still in-flight in projection projection sees EventScavenged scavenge ran while projection was behind scavenge must not remove events at or ahead of slowest projection checkpoint; check before plan execution
maxcount scavenge removes wrong events newest events removed instead of oldest off-by-one in cutoff calculation keep events with EventNumber >= current_count - max_count; unit test with exact counts

9.7 Performance Concerns

  • Recovery scan: O(last-segment-size) for torn-write detection; O(total-log-size) for full index rebuild (only if index file missing or corrupt); index rebuild for 1 TB log ~minutes — schedule at startup, serve reads only after complete.
  • CRC on read: CRC-32C with hardware acceleration (x86 _mm_crc32_u*; ARM PMULL); throughput ≥ 10 GB/s; overhead < 2% of read latency.
  • Scavenge I/O: sequential read + sequential write per segment; overlap with normal append I/O via separate I/O priority (Linux ioprio_set IDLE class); cap scavenge rate via token bucket (configurable MB/s).
  • Rename atomicity: rename(2) is O(1) metadata operation; no data-copy latency on the critical path.
  • Index rebuild memory: stream index is a BTreeMap<(StreamId, EventNumber), u64>; ~40 bytes/entry; 1B events ≈ 40 GB — shard index by stream hash bucket or use on-disk B-tree for large stores.
  • Scavenge parallelism: process multiple segments concurrently (bounded by scavenge_parallelism config, default 2) to reduce wall-clock time.

9.8 Edge Cases

  • Crash during rename: file system guarantees old segment still valid; <id>.seg.new is orphan → on open, delete all *.new files before recovery.
  • Scavenge on stream with no deletable events → plan returns empty SegmentPlan; no I/O performed; valid no-op.
  • All events in a segment are scavenged → segment file becomes empty; delete segment file + remove from manifest.
  • GlobalPosition gaps after scavenge: gaps are allowed; subscriptions see EventScavenged notification at removed positions and skip forward.
  • Recovery after scavenge + crash: scavenged segment is the new canonical; recovery scans it normally; no special case.
  • delete_stream called twice → second call: tombstone already present; return StreamAlreadyDeleted error (idempotent for scavenge planning).
  • Index rebuild finds position discontinuity → positions must be strictly increasing within segment; discontinuity = corruption; CorruptLog error + operator alert.
  • Retention policy changed after partial scavenge → re-run plan; new plan reflects updated policy; idempotent.

9.9 Failure Analysis

Failure Detection Blast Radius Recovery
Segment file unreadable (disk error) OS read error all reads to that segment fail mark segment Unreadable; return SegmentIoError for affected positions; alert; restore from backup
Index file corrupted CRC mismatch on index read all reads return wrong offsets delete index; trigger rebuild on next open
Scavenge removes live event subscription/projection sees EventScavenged for live event data loss enforce pre-scavenge projection-checkpoint check; cannot recover removed event — restore from backup
rename fails (disk full) ENOSPC on rename old segment still valid scavenge aborts; manifest not updated; old segment intact; free space and retry
Recovery truncates good data events N..M lost (false torn-write detection) data loss CRC must be computed correctly; verify with golden fixture; torn-write only truncates last partial record

9.10 Operational Notes

  • Metrics: log_recovery_duration_ms, log_torn_writes_truncated_total, log_crc_errors_total, scavenge_events_removed_total, scavenge_bytes_reclaimed_total, scavenge_duration_ms, scavenge_in_progress gauge.
  • Traces: span log.recovery, span scavenge.segment{id}.
  • Logs: INFO on recovery start/complete (events recovered, truncation offset if any); INFO on scavenge start/plan/complete; WARN on CRC mismatch; ERROR on CorruptLog, segment unreadable.
  • Scavenge scheduling: operator-initiated via esdb-cli scavenge start [--stream <id>] [--dry-run]; --dry-run prints plan without executing; future: cron-based auto-scavenge.
  • Deployment: scavenge is safe to run on a live node; --dry-run always safe; production recommendation: run scavenge off-peak; monitor scavenge_in_progress to avoid overlap.
  • Backup: take segment-level snapshot before scavenge; cloud-copy *.seg files to object storage; ScavengeManifest records which segments were compacted and when.
  • Rollback: if scavenge produces incorrect results, restore from pre-scavenge segment backup; re-run recovery; subscriptions and projections will re-catch-up from restored positions.
  • esdb-cli scavenge status shows: plan summary, bytes to reclaim, estimated duration, last scavenge time, events removed.

Phase 10 — Raft replication of the global log

10.1 Objectives

  • Wrap every committed event as ReplicationEntry { position, term, event_record } in esdb-replication.
  • Leader appends entries to its local esdb-log then replicates via AppendEntries RPC to all followers.
  • Commit index advances when a quorum of match_index values reach or exceed the entry's index.
  • Followers apply entries in order to their own esdb-log; follower log is identical to leader post-commit.
  • Only the leader accepts client appends; followers reject or redirect (I7, P6).
  • Log matching property: if two logs share an entry at (index, term), all prior entries are identical.

10.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

<!-- Theory anchors: P1 (Ongaro Raft — AppendEntries, commit_index, Log Matching), P6 (Spanner: quorum commit), I7 (committed entry never lost across leader change), B1 (Kleppmann Ch.9 — consensus and linearizability), ADR-11 (Raft chosen over Multi-Paxos), ADR-19 (bincode serialisation for replication entries) -->

esdb-replication module additions:

  • ReplicationEntry: wraps EventRecord + GlobalPosition + term: u64; serialised via bincode/prost.
  • RaftLog: append-only in-memory index of (log_index, term) pairs backed by esdb-log segment storage.
  • RaftState: { current_term, voted_for, commit_index, last_applied, role: Leader|Follower|Candidate }.
  • LeaderState: per-peer { next_index, match_index } map; initialised to leader_last_log_index + 1 / 0.
  • AppendEntries RPC handler: consistency check (prev_log_index, prev_log_term) → reject or truncate + append.
  • AppendEntriesReply: { term, success, match_index }; leader updates match_index[peer] on success.
  • Commit: after each AppendEntries reply, scan sorted match_index values; new commit_index = median of quorum.
  • apply_committed: read entries [last_applied+1 .. commit_index] from RaftLog, write to esdb-log global segment, advance last_applied, wake waiting append futures.
  • Heartbeat: empty AppendEntries sent every heartbeat_interval (default 50 ms) by leader to reset follower election timers.
  • Client append path: esdb-stream → esdb-replication::Leader::propose(events) → returns AppendResult after commit.
  • Instrumentation: per-peer replication lag (leader_last_log_index - match_index[peer]), commit latency histogram, apply throughput counter.

Files: esdb-replication/src/log.rs, state.rs, leader.rs, follower.rs, rpc.rs, apply.rs.

10.3 Deliverable

  • 3-node cluster where every committed append via leader is durably reflected on all three nodes' esdb-log.
  • cargo test -p esdb-replication passes: single-leader commit, quorum commit with one follower lagging, append rejection on follower.
  • Metric endpoint exposes replication_lag_entries{peer=...} and commit_latency_ms histogram.

10.4 Acceptance Criteria

Check Target
Quorum commit with 2/3 nodes up all appends acked
Follower rejects direct append NotLeader error + redirect
Log matching invariant property test over 10 k random entries
Commit latency (LAN, 3 nodes) p99 < 10 ms
Replication throughput ≥ 20 k events/s sustained
match_index after recovery equals commit_index within one heartbeat

10.5 Conformance Fixtures

Fixture Purpose Expected
raft_single_leader_commit.json propose 100 events, 3 nodes all commit_index equal
raft_follower_redirect.bin append RPC to follower NotLeader { leader_hint }
raft_log_matching.bin inject conflict at index 5, term mismatch follower truncates, re-syncs
raft_quorum_with_one_down.bin stop node 3, append 50 events commits succeed; node 3 catches up on rejoin
raft_torn_entry_recovery.bin crash leader mid-write follower with complete copy elected; torn entry never committed

10.6 Pitfalls

  • Stale match_index after reconnect: follower was partitioned; leader's next_index is stale; fix: decrement next_index on AppendEntries failure, binary-search rollback. Signal: replication_lag_entries spike.
  • Committed entry overwritten on follower: follower has higher-term entries from a previous brief leadership; truncation in AppendEntries handler must check term, not just index. Signal: CRC mismatch on apply.
  • Leader applies before quorum: advancing last_applied before commit_index is reached; fix: strict last_applied ≤ commit_index. Signal: client sees event that is later lost on failover.
  • Unbounded in-flight AppendEntries: pipeline without back-pressure fills TCP buffers; fix: limit per-peer in-flight count to max_pipeline (default 8). Signal: memory growth on leader.
  • Clock-skew election storm: election timeouts smaller than network RTT; fix: randomise in [150 ms, 300 ms], assert heartbeat_interval < min_election_timeout / 2.

10.7 Performance Concerns

  • Batch AppendEntries: group up to max_batch_entries=512 or max_batch_bytes=1 MiB per RPC to amortise round-trip cost.
  • Avoid per-entry fsync; group-commit: call esdb-log::flush() once per batch before sending replies.
  • match_index quorum scan: O(n peers), negligible for n ≤ 9; no heap allocation per scan.
  • Follower apply: sequential write to esdb-log; no random I/O; keep segment write buffer large (4 MiB).
  • Leadership detection in hot path: single atomic role read; never locks on read path.

10.8 Edge Cases

  • Leader receives AppendEntries with higher term → immediately transitions to Follower, abandons pending proposals.
  • Zero-event heartbeat: prev_log_index / prev_log_term consistency check still runs; must not advance commit_index spuriously.
  • GlobalPosition gap on follower apply: positions are assigned by the leader's esdb-log; follower must use leader-assigned positions, not re-derive (I3).
  • Follower log shorter than prev_log_index: reply { success: false, conflict_index: last_log_index } to accelerate rollback.
  • Re-propose after network partition: client retried; idempotent re-append by EventId dedup (I6) prevents duplicates.

10.9 Failure Analysis

Failure Detection Blast Radius Recovery
Leader crash mid-replication election timeout on followers in-flight proposals fail new leader elected; uncommitted entries rolled back on followers
Follower disk full AppendEntries reply error follower only operator expands disk; node re-syncs from leader snapshot
Network partition (minority) heartbeat timeout minority loses append access minority followers wait; rejoins, fast-forward from leader
Split-brain (bug) two leaders same term data divergence quorum check prevents; if bypassed, CRC mismatches surface on merge

10.10 Operational Notes

  • Metrics: raft_current_term, raft_role{node=...}, raft_commit_index, raft_last_applied, raft_peer_match_index{peer=...}, raft_append_entries_rpc_duration_ms.
  • Structured log: INFO raft: became leader term=7 log_index=3401; WARN raft: rejected AppendEntries term=6 < current=7.
  • Deployment: add nodes one at a time; new node starts as non-voting learner, catches up, then joins voting set.
  • Rollback: downgrade is safe as long as log format is backward-compatible; ReplicationEntry version field guards format changes.
  • Operator command: esdb-cli cluster raft-status emits term, role, commit_index, peer lag for each node.

Phase 11 — cluster membership + leader election + failover

11.1 Objectives

  • Implement full Raft leader election: terms, RequestVote RPC, randomised timeouts, vote grants.
  • Joint-consensus membership changes (AddNode/RemoveNode) to safely resize the cluster.
  • On leader loss, elect a new leader and resume appends within one election timeout cycle.
  • Followers serve stale reads with explicit ReadAtCommitIndex or redirect reads to leader.
  • Client library redirects to leader via NotLeader { leader_hint } response.
  • Split-brain prevention: a node only acts as leader if it holds a quorum grant for the current term.

11.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

<!-- Theory anchors: P1 (Ongaro sec. 3.4 leader election — randomised timeout, vote-grant rule, term monotonicity), I7 (single committed leader invariant = safety), B1 (Kleppmann split-brain and fencing), ADR-11 (Raft: election convergence bound), ADR-22 (lease-read latency vs strict quorum-read tradeoff) -->

Leader election (in esdb-replication/src/election.rs):

  • ElectionTimer: per-node Tokio timer, reset on any valid RPC; fires in [150 ms, 300 ms] uniform random.
  • On timeout: increment current_term, set voted_for = self, broadcast RequestVote { term, candidate_id, last_log_index, last_log_term }.
  • Vote grant: node votes for candidate if term > current_term AND candidate log at least as up-to-date (last_log_term > local_term OR (last_log_term == local_term AND last_log_index >= local_index)).
  • Quorum tracking: tally VoteGranted responses; on majority → transition to Leader, initialise LeaderState, send immediate heartbeat.

Membership changes (in esdb-replication/src/membership.rs):

  • ClusterConfig { voters: BTreeSet<NodeId>, learners: BTreeSet<NodeId> }.
  • Joint consensus: C_old → C_old+new (committed to both) → C_new; no two-node window with less-than-majority quorum.
  • MembershipEntry stored as a special ReplicationEntry type in the Raft log.
  • Config in effect as soon as the log entry is applied; prior config used for quorum until then.

Failover (in leader.rs / follower.rs):

  • StepDown: leader detects it hasn't communicated with quorum in step_down_timeout → resigns, becomes Follower.
  • Pre-vote (optional optimisation): candidate checks viability before incrementing term; prevents stale nodes from disrupting live cluster.
  • Follower read: ReadIndex RPC to leader returns current commit_index; follower reads up to that index for linearisable reads.

Files: esdb-replication/src/election.rs, membership.rs, config.rs; updates to state.rs, rpc.rs.

11.3 Deliverable

  • 3-node cluster survives leader kill: new leader elected, appends resume within 500 ms.
  • esdb-cli cluster status shows current leader, term, member list, per-node commit index.
  • Membership change: esdb-cli cluster add-node <addr> completes without downtime (joint consensus).

11.4 Acceptance Criteria

Check Target
Leader election after SIGKILL new leader within 2× max election timeout
No two leaders in same term property test, 100 k steps, chaos inject
Membership change (3→5 nodes) zero downtime; no append rejected
Stale read caveat documented followers return commit_index in response
Vote idempotency re-sent RequestVote same term does not change vote
RequestVote rejected for stale log up-to-date check prevents stale leader

11.5 Conformance Fixtures

Fixture Purpose Expected
election_basic_3node.sim kill leader at T=0 new leader by T=600 ms
election_split_vote.sim 3 candidates same term at most one leader per term
membership_add_node.sim grow 3→5 during load joint consensus, no lost commits
membership_remove_leader.sim remove the current leader node leader steps down, new elected
stale_read_linearisable.bin follower read before catch-up stale_at_index caveat in response
pre_vote_disruption.sim partitioned node rejoins with high term pre-vote prevents term inflation

11.6 Pitfalls

  • Disruptive re-join with inflated term: a partitioned node increments term many times; rejoining forces a spurious election. Mitigation: pre-vote or CheckQuorum step-down. Signal: frequent became_follower log events.
  • Asymmetric partition (leader can send, not receive): leader keeps sending heartbeats; followers elect a new leader; old leader thinks it's still active. Fix: CheckQuorum — leader steps down if it has not heard from quorum in one election timeout. Signal: two leaders detected by client redirects.
  • Joint-consensus deadlock: new node added but its log is far behind; quorum of C_old+new blocks commit. Fix: learner mode with max_catch_up_rounds before promoting to voter.
  • Vote split on every election: all nodes timeout at same time. Fix: jitter in [150 ms, 300 ms]; seed per node-id for determinism in tests.
  • Membership entry applied before it is committed: node uses C_new quorum before the entry is committed in C_old. Fix: config in effect only after entry is applied, not when it enters the log.

11.7 Performance Concerns

  • Election timer reset: single atomic write per incoming RPC; zero contention.
  • RequestVote broadcast: O(n) RPCs in parallel; futures joined with timeout; no sequential fan-out.
  • Membership change overhead: extra round-trip for joint consensus; infrequent; not on hot path.
  • Step-down check: timer-based, no lock; fired at heartbeat interval.
  • Follower ReadIndex RPC: one extra round-trip per linearisable follower read; cache commit_index at heartbeat frequency to amortise.

11.8 Edge Cases

  • Network heals while election in progress: stale RequestVote from previous term received by new leader → reject (term < current_term), no state change.
  • Candidate receives AppendEntries with equal term from a real leader → revert to Follower (leader already elected this term).
  • Single-node cluster: quorum = 1; node is always leader; no election needed; membership change to 3 nodes requires pre-adding two learners.
  • Node ID collision on re-IP: must use stable node IDs (UUID or config-assigned); warn on startup if ID conflicts detected.
  • Clock skew causing false heartbeat timeouts: election timeout must be >> network RTT; document minimum election_timeout_min = 5 × heartbeat_interval.

11.9 Failure Analysis

Failure Detection Blast Radius Recovery
All nodes crash simultaneously external health check full outage restart majority; Raft recovers committed log
Persistent network partition (equal halves) no majority quorum no appends operator manually rejoin or adjust config
Zombie leader (CheckQuorum off) client sees two leaders split-brain risk enable CheckQuorum; leader steps down
Bad membership change (removes quorum) commit hangs cluster frozen operator force-add node via admin endpoint

11.10 Operational Notes

  • Metrics: raft_elections_total, raft_leader_changes_total, raft_term, raft_cluster_size{role=voter|learner}, raft_step_down_total.
  • Alert: raft_leader_changes_total rate > 1/min suggests instability.
  • esdb-cli cluster raft-status: prints term, leader, voters, learners, commit_index per node.
  • Deployment: add nodes as learners first; promote after max_catch_up_rounds; remove only non-leader nodes without disrupting quorum.
  • Config file cluster.toml: node_id, peers, election_timeout_min_ms, election_timeout_max_ms, heartbeat_interval_ms.
  • Upgrade: rolling restart is safe if log format is stable; term number persists across restarts in current_term.dat (single-value file, fsync on write).

Phase 12 — partitioning / sharding

12.1 Objectives

  • Introduce esdb-partition: maps StreamId to a partition via consistent hashing; each partition is owned by a Raft group or a single node.
  • Route Append and stream Read RPCs to the owning node; return WrongNode { owner } on mismatch.
  • $all global log is a merge of per-partition global logs ordered by GlobalPosition.
  • Rebalancing on membership change: ownership transfered without losing or duplicating events.
  • Category and stream affinity: streams in the same category (prefix-) can be pinned to the same partition.
  • Cross-partition queries read $all via a merge cursor (or per-partition fan-out).

12.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

<!-- Theory anchors: P4 (Helland: per-partition position spaces — I3 applies per-shard), ADR-23 (per-partition GlobalPosition), P6 (Spanner: multi-shard commit — shard-local Raft + coordinator), B1 (Kleppmann Ch.6 partitioning — consistent hash rebalancing), ADR-25 (xxhash3_64 stream routing key), I3 (commit positions monotonic within each partition) -->

esdb-partition/src/ring.rs — consistent hash ring:

  • HashRing { vnodes: BTreeMap<u64, PartitionId>, replication_factor: u8 }.
  • StreamId → hash via xxhash3_64(stream_id.as_bytes()) → walk ring to first PartitionId.
  • PartitionId → NodeId via PartitionMap { partition: BTreeMap<PartitionId, NodeId> }.
  • Category affinity: hash on category(stream_id) prefix for streams sharing a category (configurable).

esdb-partition/src/router.rs — routing layer:

  • Router: intercepts AppendRequest / ReadRequest; resolves owner via HashRing + PartitionMap.
  • On local ownership: pass through to esdb-stream.
  • On remote ownership: forward RPC via esdb-server client stub; retry on WrongNode with updated routing table.
  • RoutingTable: versioned (epoch: u64); atomically swapped on rebalance; reader-writer lock.

esdb-partition/src/rebalance.rs — rebalancing:

  • Trigger: node join/leave event from Raft membership change.
  • Plan: compute new HashRing; diff old vs new → set of (partition, old_owner, new_owner) moves.
  • Transfer: old owner streams events for partition via TransferStream RPC to new owner; new owner replays into local esdb-log; atomic cut-over swaps PartitionMap entry at a known GlobalPosition.
  • Safety: during transfer, both old and new owner accept reads; only old owner accepts writes (fencing via partition lease).

esdb-partition/src/all_merge.rs — $all cross-partition cursor:

  • AllMergeCursor { per_partition_cursors: Vec<ReadCursor>, heap: BinaryHeap<(GlobalPosition, PartitionId)> }.
  • Merge-sorts by GlobalPosition; ties broken by PartitionId for determinism.
  • Handles partition gaps: if a partition has no events in a range, its cursor returns is_end=true; merger skips.

Files: esdb-partition/src/{ring,router,rebalance,all_merge,lease,config}.rs.

12.3 Deliverable

  • 5-node cluster with 4 partitions; esdb-cli cluster partition-map shows stream → partition → node.
  • StreamId-based routing verified: appends to stream order-123 always land on the same node.
  • Rebalance test: add a 6th node; partitions migrate without event loss or duplication.
  • $all read across partitions returns events in GlobalPosition order.

12.4 Acceptance Criteria

Check Target
Correct owner resolution 100 k random StreamIds; zero misroute
WrongNode redirect overhead < 1 extra RTT on stale routing table
Rebalance event loss zero; property test over 10 k events during rebalance
$all merge order monotonically increasing GlobalPosition; property test
Category affinity all streams with same category land on same partition
Routing table update latency < 100 ms after membership change propagated

12.5 Conformance Fixtures

Fixture Purpose Expected
partition_routing_stable.json 1000 streams, 4 partitions deterministic assignment matches ring hash
partition_rebalance_3to4.sim grow 3→4 partitions all events present post-rebalance, no duplicates
partition_wrong_node.bin send append to non-owner WrongNode { owner } response
partition_all_merge_order.bin 4 partitions, interleaved positions merge output strictly increasing
partition_affinity_category.json streams order-* pinned to partition 0 all in same partition
partition_lease_fencing.sim dual-owner during transfer only original owner writes; new owner read-only until cut-over

12.6 Pitfalls

  • Stale routing table causes redirect loop: node A routes to B, B's routing table routes back to A; fix: include routing_epoch in WrongNode reply; client refreshes table and retries once, not indefinitely. Signal: redirect_loop_detected counter.
  • Split-partition $all positions not globally unique: two partitions independently assign overlapping GlobalPosition values; fix: partition-scoped positions + merge key includes PartitionId, OR leader assigns positions from a shared monotonic counter (preferred). Signal: duplicate positions in merge output.
  • Rebalance transfer races with live appends: new events arrive on old owner during transfer; fix: old owner applies new events to transfer stream before fencing; cut-over position must be ≥ last transferred position. Signal: events missing from new owner post-cut-over.
  • Category affinity skew: one category has millions of streams; one partition overloaded. Mitigation: sub-partition by hash within category when partition event count exceeds threshold.
  • Learner node promoted too early: receives partition ownership before it has replicated all events; fix: rebalance only to nodes with commit_index within max_lag_entries of leader.

12.7 Performance Concerns

  • Hash ring lookup: O(log n) BTreeMap seek, sub-microsecond; cache last-used (StreamId, PartitionId) pair per connection.
  • Routing table RWLock: short critical section (pointer swap); readers use Arc snapshot, no lock contention on hot path.
  • $all merge heap: O(P log P) per event where P = partition count; for P ≤ 64, heap fits in one cache line.
  • Rebalance transfer: streaming copy avoids full snapshot; rate-limit to rebalance_bandwidth_mib_s (default 100 MiB/s) to protect foreground latency.
  • Cross-partition fan-out for read-by-category: parallelize; await all partition reads concurrently; merge locally.

12.8 Edge Cases

  • Partition count = 1: degenerates to single-node (or single-Raft-group) mode; all routing is local; $all merge has one cursor.
  • Node hosting multiple partitions: PartitionMap allows M:1; no constraint violation; rebalance moves one partition at a time.
  • StreamId with no - separator: category = full StreamId; consistent hash still works; no special casing needed.
  • GlobalPosition wrap-around at u64::MAX: theoretical limit ~1.8 × 10^19 events; document, assert, and add a metric global_position_hwm.
  • Concurrent rebalances: disallow; queue rebalance requests; execute serially under a cluster-level rebalance lock held by the Raft leader.
  • Follower receives routed append: must reject with NotLeader; routing layer must distinguish partition-ownership from Raft leadership.

12.9 Failure Analysis

Failure Detection Blast Radius Recovery
Partition owner node crashes health check + WrongNode errors partition unavailable until Raft elects new leader Raft elects new leader for that partition group; resumes < 2 election timeouts
Rebalance stalls mid-transfer rebalance_duration_s alert writes to migrating partition paused operator cancels transfer (esdb-cli cluster cancel-rebalance); old owner retakes ownership
Hash ring corruption (bug) streams route to wrong partition wrong-node errors, data misrouted recompute ring from cluster.toml; verify with partition-map command
Partition skew (one node overloaded) partition_event_count imbalance metric hot partition latency degrades trigger manual rebalance; adjust vnode count for skewed category

12.10 Operational Notes

  • Metrics: partition_count, partition_owner{partition=...}, partition_event_count{partition=...}, routing_redirects_total, rebalance_duration_seconds, rebalance_events_transferred_total.
  • Alert: routing_redirects_total rate > 100/s indicates stale routing tables across the fleet.
  • esdb-cli cluster partition-map: tabular output of PartitionId → NodeId → event_count → lag_entries.
  • esdb-cli cluster rebalance --dry-run: prints planned moves without executing.
  • Config: partition_count in cluster.toml is set at cluster init and immutable without a full rebalance; document in runbook.
  • Compatibility: RoutingTable is versioned by epoch; old clients without partition support route all traffic to node 0 (single-partition fallback, degraded mode, logged as WARN).

Phase 13 — Server API + Competing Consumers + Backpressure

13.1 Objectives

  • Expose all store operations over gRPC (primary) and HTTP/JSON (secondary) in esdb-server.
  • Implement persistent subscription groups: competing consumers sharing a subscription, each ack/nack independently.
  • Enforce at-least-once delivery with park, retry, and dead-letter queues.
  • Flow-control: per-consumer window (max-in-flight), stream-level backpressure from esdb-subscribe to the server send loop.
  • Auth: per-request token validation (JWT or mTLS); coarse ACL per stream category.
  • Cluster gossip: nodes exchange membership state, health, and leader identity.
  • Constraints: gRPC streaming cancellation must drain acks cleanly; partition routing transparent to clients.
  • Scope: esdb-server, esdb-subscribe (persistent group layer), esdb-cli sub-commands subscribe / ack / nack.

13.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

<!-- Theory anchors: P3 (OCC at API boundary — ExpectedVersion CAS exposed as gRPC field), S3 (protobuf serialisation — wire format compatibility), I5 (WrongExpectedVersion propagated as gRPC Status::FAILED_PRECONDITION), ADR-02 (gRPC+protobuf over REST/JSON), ADR-08 (semantic versioning for wire format), B1 (Kleppmann Ch.12: API backward-compat under rolling upgrade) -->

Proto / API surface (esdb-server/proto/esdb.proto): Append(AppendReq)→AppendResp, Read(ReadReq)→stream EventRecord, Subscribe(SubscribeReq)→stream SubscriptionEvent, Ack(AckReq)→AckResp, Nack(NackReq)→NackResp, CreateSubscriptionGroup(GroupReq)→GroupResp, Project(ProjectReq)→ProjectResp, ScavengeStart(ScavengeReq)→ScavengeResp, ClusterInfo(Empty)→ClusterInfoResp; HTTP gateway via grpc-gateway annotations on Append/Read/Subscribe/ClusterInfo for REST clients.

Competing consumers (esdb-server/src/competing.rs + esdb-subscribe/src/group.rs): ConsumerGroup { group_id: Uuid, filter: SubscriptionFilter, checkpoint: Checkpoint, max_in_flight: usize, ack_timeout: Duration, max_retries: u32, in_flight: BTreeMap<GlobalPosition, InFlightRecord>, parked: VecDeque<EventRecord>, consumers: HashMap<ConsumerId, ConsumerState> }, with InFlightRecord { event: EventRecord, consumer_id: ConsumerId, dispatched_at: Instant, retry_count: u32 }. Dispatch is configurable — round-robin (default) or least-in-flight (DispatchStrategy enum). Ack removes from in_flight and, if position == contiguous frontier, advances the checkpoint to the next unacked position and persists it. Nack(Retry) increments retry_count and, while under max_retries, re-queues to the front of parked with exponential backoff (base_ms * 2^retry_count, capped at max_backoff_ms), else parks as dead-letter; Nack(Park) moves the event to dead-letter stream $dead-letter-<group_id> via normal esdb-stream::append (no requeue); Nack(Skip) discards and advances as if acked. A tokio::interval timeout sweep at ack_timeout / 4 scans in_flight from the smallest position, evicts entries older than ack_timeout, and requeues them as Nack(Retry). Checkpoint writes batch every checkpoint_interval_ms or checkpoint_batch_size acks via esdb-storage::put_checkpoint; group config persists at key group:<group_id> and survives restart.

Backpressure (esdb-server/src/backpressure.rs): ConsumerSemaphore(Arc<Semaphore>) holds max_in_flight permits — the dispatch loop acquires one per event sent, ack/nack/timeout releases it; esdb-subscribe delivers via tokio::sync::mpsc::channel(cap = 2 * max_in_flight), and the pull loop's channel.recv() naturally parks when full; HTTP/2 flow control propagates automatically via tonic once the TCP send buffer fills (no extra action needed on the HTTP path). Slow-consumer detection: permit wait beyond slow_consumer_warn_ms emits WARN + increments slow_consumer_events_total{consumer_id}; dead consumers (no ack within consumer_dead_timeout) are removed from the group and their in-flight events requeued.

Auth (esdb-server/src/auth.rs): an interceptor extracts Authorization: Bearer <token> from gRPC metadata or client_certificate from the mTLS layer; JWT signatures validate against a cached JWKS endpoint (refreshed on exp approach), extracting sub/roles into AuthContext; an ACL table Vec<AclEntry { pattern: GlobPattern, required_role: Role, operations: PermSet }> loads from auth.toml and is checked in order, first match wins, where PermSet covers Append/Read/Subscribe/Admin. Unauthenticated → Status::unauthenticated; insufficient role → Status::permission_denied; every PERMISSION_DENIED is audit-logged at WARN with principal, target_stream, operation.

Cluster gossip (esdb-server/src/gossip.rs): GossipMessage { node_id: NodeId, role: Role, commit_index: u64, epoch: u64, grpc_endpoint: SocketAddr, http_endpoint: SocketAddr, sent_at: Instant } fans out to all known peers every gossip_interval_ms over existing gRPC connections; GossipTable { entries: HashMap<NodeId, GossipMessage>, last_updated: Instant } evicts entries after 3 * gossip_interval_ms without update; /health returns { node_id, role, commit_index, peers: [..] } JSON for load-balancer checks; a follower receiving Append redirects with Status::failed_precondition + metadata x-esdb-leader-endpoint: <addr>; two nodes both reporting Leader in the same term emits WARN metric gossip_split_detected_total.

Connection lifecycle: tonic::transport::Server with ctrl_c/SIGTERM triggering graceful shutdown — stop accepting new connections, wait up to shutdown_grace_ms for in-flight streams, then force-close; keep-alive http2_keepalive_interval = 20s / http2_keepalive_timeout = 5s stops proxies silently dropping idle subscribe streams; a generated connection UUID is logged at DEBUG on connect/disconnect for correlation.

HTTP REST gateway (esdb-server/src/http.rs): an axum router wraps the same service handlers as gRPC over routes POST /streams/{stream}/events, GET /streams/{stream}/events, GET /all, GET /health, GET /cluster; JSON bodies map 1:1 to proto fields, with EventRecord.data base64-encoded.

13.3 Deliverable

  • esdb-server binary starts; registers with cluster; serves gRPC Append/Read/Subscribe/Project and HTTP REST.
  • Persistent subscription group with three competing consumers: round-robin dispatch, ack, nack-park, dead-letter stream visible via esdb-cli read.
  • Backpressure observable: slow consumer causes permit contention; fast producer's p99 bounded; no OOM.
  • /health returns current role + commit_index; ClusterInfo RPC returns full peer list.
  • Auth middleware rejects unauthorized requests with correct gRPC status codes; audit log written.
  • esdb-cli subscribe --group <id> --ack | --nack-park functional.

13.4 Acceptance Criteria

Check Target
cargo test -p esdb-server --test integration all pass
Competing-consumer integration: 3 consumers, 1000 events each event acked exactly once; no duplicates
Nack-park + retry cycle event appears in dead-letter stream after max_retries exceeded
Backpressure: slow consumer, 10k events server RSS growth < 50 MB; no event lost
Auth: no-token request UNAUTHENTICATED
Auth: wrong-category token PERMISSION_DENIED; audit log entry written
Gossip convergence: kill leader, 3-node cluster new leader gossip-visible within 3 × gossip_interval_ms
Graceful shutdown during active subscribe stream receives clean end or UNAVAILABLE within shutdown_grace_ms
HTTP REST: POST /streams/{s}/events equivalent to gRPC Append; same AppendResult
Leader redirect follower returns failed_precondition + leader endpoint header

13.5 Conformance Fixtures

File Purpose Expected outcome
fixtures/competing_ack_nack.toml 3 consumers, interleaved ack/nack/park checkpoint = highest contiguously acked position
fixtures/backpressure_slow_consumer.toml consumer stalls 500 ms between acks; max_in_flight=10 server channel blocks at 10 in-flight; no OOM; no drop
fixtures/dead_letter_cycle.toml nack-park max_retries times on same event event present in $dead-letter-<group>; not re-dispatched
fixtures/auth_acl_matrix.toml 4 token/operation combinations (valid/invalid × allowed/denied) correct status code for each cell
fixtures/gossip_leader_failover.toml 3 nodes; leader killed at t=5s new leader elected; client redirect header present in subsequent Append
fixtures/graceful_shutdown_subscribe.toml shutdown sent during active stream subscription stream ends cleanly; no orphan goroutines; pid exits within grace period
fixtures/http_rest_parity.toml same append/read sequence via HTTP and gRPC byte-equal EventRecord output

13.6 Pitfalls

Pitfall Manifestation Root cause Debug signal Recovery
Checkpoint regression on partial ack batch duplicate events after restart checkpoint advanced before contiguous frontier reached checkpoint_lag_positions metric spikes after restart checkpoint only at contiguous frontier; never skip gaps
Dead-letter projection loop dead-letter stream triggers same projection, events re-park projection subscribed to $all, catches dead-letter events nack_park_total grows unboundedly filter projection to exclude $dead-letter-* by event-type or stream prefix
Gossip split (two leaders visible) minority nodes redirect clients to wrong endpoint network partition + simultaneous timeout gossip_split_detected_total > 0 Raft quorum prevents dual commit; gossip role is advisory; no data hazard
gRPC stream leak after TCP RST active_streams metric grows without bound TCP RST not forwarded through NAT/proxy no keepalive pings, stream never cleaned enable http2_keepalive_interval; server-side ping timeout evicts stale streams
In-flight map memory growth OOM on server under sustained nack storms acks never received from crashed consumers in_flight_count{group} gauge; heap_bytes process metric ack timeout sweep + max_in_flight cap per group; dead-consumer eviction
Competing-consumer rebalance storm all events re-dispatched on consumer join all in-flight timed out simultaneously at same moment nack_timeout_total spike stagger ack timeouts with jitter; use consistent-hash dispatch to reduce reassignment
JWT JWKS refresh race brief window of auth failures after key rotation in-flight requests use old key after cache invalidates auth_failure_total spike coinciding with JWKS refresh accept both old and new keys during clock_skew_tolerance overlap window

13.7 Performance Concerns

  • Ack path fsync: checkpoint write synchronous on hot path; batch every N acks or T ms to amortize; target < 1 fsync per 50 acks.
  • In-flight BTreeMap scan: timeout sweep O(n) walk; use BTreeMap ordered by dispatched_at-mapped key; evict from front in O(log n).
  • Backpressure semaphore contention: shared Arc<Semaphore> under 100+ concurrent consumers; shard per consumer-slot or use per-consumer channel with bounded capacity.
  • gRPC framing overhead: small events pay high per-message cost; buffer up to 1 ms or 100 events before flushing subscribe stream; expose subscribe_batch_max config.
  • Gossip fan-out at scale: O(n²) TCP sends above 20 nodes; switch to epidemic push-pull gossip; use rand::shuffle of peer list per round.
  • Auth middleware: JWT RSA verify is CPU-heavy; cache verified tokens in moka::Cache keyed by token hash; TTL = min(token_exp, 60 s).
  • HTTP REST baseline: axum + serde_json encode/decode adds ~10 µs per request vs gRPC+prost; acceptable for control-plane use; not the hot path.

13.8 Edge Cases

  • Consumer connects mid-replay: must receive events from checkpoint position, not live head; subscribe catch-up logic from Phase 7 handles this.
  • Two consumers ack the same event (timeout + late ack): second ack is a no-op; idempotent removal from in_flight BTreeMap.
  • Leader failover during active Append RPC: client receives UNAVAILABLE; retries with same EventIds; new leader deduplicates via I6.
  • Persistent subscription group created while stream has 10M historical events: catch-up must page (1000 events/batch), not load all into memory.
  • HTTP/2 RST_STREAM mid-batch: partial permit window released; affected events requeued by timeout sweep, not lost.
  • Dead-letter stream unbounded growth: enforce $maxcount metadata on $dead-letter-<group_id> stream; scavenge applies.
  • Consumer sends ack for event not in group's in-flight map (stale after restart): return AckResp { status: NOT_IN_FLIGHT }; no state mutation.
  • Auth token with future nbf claim: reject with UNAUTHENTICATED; log nbf value and server clock for clock-skew diagnosis.

13.9 Failure Analysis

Failure Detection Blast radius Containment Recovery
esdb-server process crash health check fails all active subscribe streams lost persistent subscriptions resume from checkpoint on restart restart; checkpoint replay in esdb-subscribe
Auth service JWKS endpoint unreachable JWT verify timeout all new connections rejected circuit breaker: allow cached tokens up to jwks_staleness_tolerance restore JWKS endpoint; cache auto-refreshes
Competing group checkpoint corrupted duplicate events replayed after restart one group's read model re-create group from last known-good position esdb-cli subscribe reset-group --group <id> --from <pos>
Port exhausted (ephemeral) EADDRINUSE on accept new connections fail rate-limit reconnect attempts; use SO_REUSEPORT increase port range via net.ipv4.ip_local_port_range
Gossip partition (minority) minority nodes see stale leader minority nodes redirect to wrong endpoint Raft quorum prevents minority commits partition heals; gossip reconverges within 3 × gossip_interval_ms
Dead-letter stream write failure nack_park returns error events re-dispatched indefinitely cap retry count anyway; log error at ERROR investigate esdb-stream append errors on dead-letter stream

13.10 Operational Notes

  • Metrics (prometheus): append_requests_total, append_latency_seconds{p50,p99}, in_flight_count{group}, nack_total{group,reason}, checkpoint_lag_positions{group}, active_streams{kind}, gossip_peers_seen, auth_failure_total{reason}, slow_consumer_events_total{consumer_id}.
  • Tracing: inject trace_id from gRPC metadata x-trace-id; propagate through esdb-stream → esdb-log via tracing::Span; emit span on every ack/nack with group + position.
  • Logging: structured JSON via tracing-subscriber; ack/nack at DEBUG; park/dead-letter at WARN; auth failure at WARN with principal; crash at ERROR with stack.
  • Deployment: rolling restart safe; in-flight events requeued by ack_timeout sweep; no quorum loss if one node restarted at a time.
  • Config knobs: max_in_flight, ack_timeout_ms, checkpoint_interval_ms, checkpoint_batch_size, max_retries, max_backoff_ms, shutdown_grace_ms, gossip_interval_ms, jwks_url, jwks_staleness_tolerance_ms.
  • Dead-letter inspection: esdb-cli read --stream '$dead-letter-<group_id>'; fields include retry_count in metadata.
  • Auth key rotation: publish new JWKS key alongside old; wait clock_skew_tolerance before revoking old; server accepts both during overlap.
  • HTTP REST: /health and /cluster require no auth; all data-plane endpoints require auth; rate-limit with tower::limit::RateLimitLayer.

Phase 14 — Hardening + Replay Conformance + Perf + Packing

14.1 Objectives

  • Prove correctness via four test families: replay-equivalence, optimistic-concurrency race fuzz, Raft safety under partition, crash-recovery fuzz on the log.
  • Establish append/read throughput and p99 latency baselines; apply profiling-guided tuning to meet targets.
  • Produce a backup/export pack: a consistent, portable snapshot of the global log usable for DR or migration.
  • Constraints: no new production features; all changes are tests, fixtures, benchmarks, or performance tuning within existing module boundaries.
  • Scope: tests/ integration corpus, benches/, esdb-storage/src/pack.rs, esdb-cli sub-commands pack export | verify | import.

14.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

<!-- Theory anchors: I9 (snap+tail == full_replay — this phase's primary invariant), I8 (deterministic projection replay), I2 (gap-free EventNumber — regression corpus checks this end-to-end), I4 (all-or-nothing append — chaos injection verifies atomicity), P8 (Crash-Only Recovery — chaos harness validates recovery path), B1 (Kleppmann sec. Appendix C: lineage of end-to-end tests for distributed systems) -->

Replay-equivalence harness (tests/replay_conformance/mod.rs): for each (stream_id, snapshot_interval, events) in the corpus, compute full_replay_state: Bytes by folding all events from EventNumber(0) through the projection handler and snapshot_plus_tail_state: Bytes by loading the nearest Snapshot at or before the target version then folding tail events, asserting byte equality; for each projection in fixtures/projection_rebuild/, run in Continuous mode to position P, record read_model_state, call projection.rebuild(), replay 0→P, and assert rebuilt_state == incremental_state. Property test prop_replay_equiv(stream_len in 1usize..5000, snap_every in 1usize..500) explores both paths; the projection handler contract requires a pure function (state, event) -> state with no side effects or wall-clock reads, enforced by a DeterministicEnv wrapper that panics on disallowed syscalls.

Optimistic-concurrency race fuzz (tests/concurrency_fuzz/mod.rs): N writer tasks (N in 2..=20 via proptest) each append to the same StreamId with ExpectedVersion::Exact(k) for their own expected version; results (Ok(AppendResult) / Err(WrongExpectedVersion)) are checked for gap-free event numbers, an exact union of successfully appended EventIds against stream contents, no duplicated EventId, and no skipped version. A loom model wraps the esdb-stream in-process path (segment + stream index) and enumerates interleavings up to loom::MAX_PREEMPTIONS = 3; a real-I/O fuzz variant runs 10k proptest iterations on a TempDir-backed log, checking invariants after each batch.

Raft safety fuzz (tests/raft_safety/mod.rs): a simulated network (esdb-replication/src/sim_net.rs) implements the Transport trait with injectable PartitionPolicy { drop_prob, delay_range, partition_set }, driving named deterministic scenarios leader_isolate, symmetric_partition, simultaneous_candidates, follower_crash_mid_replicate, network_heal_after_5_terms. After each scenario + heal, committed_log is compared across all nodes to assert no committed entry is missing on any node that was in quorum, no two nodes hold conflicting entries at the same index, and commit_index only advances, never regresses; a quickcheck generator runs 500 random (time, action: Partition|Heal|Crash|Restart) schedules, cross-checked against the informational TLA+ spec in docs/raft.tla (not executed in CI).

Crash-recovery fuzz (tests/crash_fuzz/mod.rs): FaultFs (esdb-log/src/fault_fs.rs) wraps std::fs::File, intercepting every write + fsync, and at a configurable kill_at write-count returns an I/O error and sets a process-level KILLED flag. The fuzz loop runs, for each kill_at in 1..=total_writes, an append sequence to kill_at, records commit_index_before_kill, restarts the node against the same TempDir, and verifies CRC validity on all recovered records, no torn tail beyond commit_index_before_kill, bit-exact presence of every event at or below that position, and absence of any event above it; failing kill_at values + corpus are stored as binary seeds in fixtures/crash_fuzz_seeds.bin.

Throughput + p99 benchmarks (benches/throughput.rs, benches/read.rs): bench_append_sequential (single stream, 100-event batches, 10k iterations, criterion p50/p99/p999), bench_append_concurrent (100 streams × 10 writers, aggregate events/sec), bench_read_all_forward ($all, 1M events, MB/s), bench_subscription_catchup (subscribe from 0 on a 1M-event log), bench_projection_rebuild (100k-event rebuild). Targets:

Benchmark Target
Sequential append (single node) ≥ 50k events/sec, p99 < 5 ms
Concurrent append (100 streams, 10 writers) ≥ 200k events/sec aggregate
$all forward read ≥ 200 MB/s
Subscription catch-up ≥ 100k events/sec
Projection rebuild ≥ 80k events/sec

Profiling + tuning actions: cargo flamegraph --bench bench_append_sequential -- --bench identifies the top-3 hot paths; likely bottlenecks and mitigations:

Bottleneck Mitigation
fsync per batch accumulate N events or T µs; issue single fsync; target ≤ 2 fsyncs per 100-event batch
Segment pre-allocation stall background task pre-allocates next segment at 80% current capacity via fallocate
prost encode/decode profile; if > 15% CPU, evaluate zero-copy frame for internal path
$all read syscall overhead mmap + MADV_SEQUENTIAL for large sequential reads; O_DIRECT for export
Index B-tree lookup per append LRU cache last-N (stream, number) -> offset entries; size = 4× active stream count
BytesMut allocations on append pool of pre-allocated BytesMut in append hot path; return to pool after fsync

Backup/export pack (esdb-storage/src/pack.rs): PackHeader { magic: [u8;8], version: u16, node_id: Uuid, exported_at: i64, from_position: u64, to_position: u64, event_count: u64, crc32c: u32 }; PackFrame { length: u32, crc32c: u32, data: EventRecord (prost-encoded) } (same framing as the segment log); PackTrailer { index_offset: u64, index_entries: Vec<PackIndexEntry { position: u64, file_offset: u64 }> }. PackWriter::new(path, from, to) -> Self opens the file, writes the header, iterates the global log from→to, and writes frames + trailer + index; PackReader::open(path) -> Result<Self> verifies the header magic, loads the trailer index into memory, and exposes read_event(pos) -> Option<EventRecord>; pack_verify(path) -> Result<VerifyReport { frames_ok, frames_corrupt, missing_positions }> runs a full CRC scan; pack_import(path, store) -> Result<ImportStats> replays frames in order via esdb-stream::append with ExpectedVersion::Any, using EventId for re-import idempotency. CLI: esdb-cli pack export --output <file> [--from <pos>] [--to <pos>], pack verify <file>, pack import --input <file> [--target <endpoint>].

14.3 Deliverable

  • All four fuzz/conformance test families pass in CI without flakes.
  • cargo bench produces bench_report.json with p50/p99/p999 per scenario; values meet targets above.
  • esdb-cli pack export produces valid .esdbpack; pack verify passes; pack import bootstraps a new node that passes all conformance checks.
  • Flamegraph SVGs committed to docs/perf/ for each benchmark on milestone release.
  • docs/conformance.md lists all conformance fixtures and their pass criteria (informational; generated from test output, not hand-written).

14.4 Acceptance Criteria

Check Target
Replay-equivalence: full vs snapshot+tail pass for all (stream_len, snap_every) in proptest corpus (10k iterations)
Projection rebuild == incremental pass for all projection types, 10k-event corpus
Concurrency fuzz: gap-free event numbers zero violations over 10k proptest iterations
Loom concurrency: no data race loom model explores to completion without failure
Raft safety: no committed event lost zero violations over 500 quickcheck partition schedules
Raft safety: no dual leader in same term zero violations
Crash-recovery: consistent log after kill zero CRC errors, zero missing committed events over full kill_at range
Sequential append throughput ≥ 50k events/sec
Sequential append p99 < 5 ms (single node, no replication)
$all read bandwidth ≥ 200 MB/s sequential
Pack round-trip export → verify → import; all events present; positions preserved; CRC valid
Pack truncated file pack verify returns error; pack import aborts; no partial state written

14.5 Conformance Fixtures

File Purpose Expected outcome
fixtures/replay_equiv_corpus/ 20 streams, varied lengths (100–50k) + snapshot intervals full replay == snapshot+tail for every stream + projection
fixtures/projection_rebuild/ 5 projection types, 10k events each rebuild state byte-equal to incremental state
fixtures/concurrency_race_seeds.bin proptest replay seeds that previously exposed violations all pass; zero regressions
fixtures/raft_partition_schedules.json 50 named + random partition scenarios commit logs agree on all nodes after heal
fixtures/crash_fuzz_seeds.bin kill-point seeds with known pre-crash commit positions log consistent; all committed events present; no extras
fixtures/pack_golden.esdbpack known-good export of 100k events pack verify passes; import produces identical read output
fixtures/pack_truncated.esdbpack truncated mid-frame (last 100 bytes removed) pack verify returns FrameCorrupt error; import aborts cleanly
fixtures/pack_duplicate_import.esdbpack same 1k events imported twice second import idempotent; event count unchanged; no errors

14.6 Pitfalls

Pitfall Manifestation Root cause Debug signal Recovery
Replay non-determinism projection rebuild yields different state handler reads wall-clock or rand state diff on rebuild vs incremental run enforce DeterministicEnv wrapper; panic on banned syscall
loom state-space explosion loom test does not complete in 1 hour too many concurrent tasks in model reduce task count to 3; use loom::MAX_PREEMPTIONS = 2 shrink concurrency scope; extract minimal reproducer
Crash-fuzz false positive torn tail flagged as missing committed event oracle uses append count, not commit_index compare against Raft commit_index snapshotted before kill record commit_index in fuzz state struct before FaultFs kill
Pack export too large for DR timeline full export takes hours on large store no position range filter no --from/--to specified use recent window; combine with snapshot bootstrap for older history
Benchmark variance masks regression p99 fluctuates ±30% between runs OS scheduling, page-cache cold start run on isolated cores (taskset); warm cache before measure use criterion outlier detection; require 5 runs; fail on > 20% regression
Index cache too small for read bench lower-than-expected $all throughput working set exceeds LRU capacity index_cache_miss_total counter increase index_cache_capacity in bench config; re-run
Scavenge racing with pack export exported pack contains gaps scavenge deletes events during export pack verify detects position gaps take scavenge lock during export or export from immutable snapshot position

14.7 Performance Concerns

  • I/O path: fsync dominates append p99 — target < 2 fsyncs per 100-event batch, confirm via strace -e trace=fsync; segment roll-over fallocate can take 10–50 ms on spinning disk, so pre-allocate in background once a segment hits 80% capacity; pack export pollutes the page cache — use O_DIRECT for the pack write path with BufWriter<AlignedWriter> at 4 KB alignment.
  • CPU/memory path: mmap + MADV_SEQUENTIAL outperforms pread for $all scans above ~4 MB (break-even ~100 events at 1 KB/event) — benchmark both; prost encode/decode is measured with divan, and if > 15% CPU, profile field ordering or consider capnproto for internal framing; subscription catch-up pulls 1000 events per esdb-log::read_batch to amortize per-event overhead, tuned against tcp_wmem; projection state should use an arena or one Bytes::copy_from_slice, avoiding per-event Vec::clone.

14.8 Edge Cases

  • Replay correctness: a stream's tombstone appears to the projection as a normal EventRecord (event_type = "$streamDeleted") and rebuild must match incremental (I8); a snapshot at exact stream end has an empty tail, so snapshot_plus_tail trivially equals full_replay (I9) — the fixture corpus must include this case.
  • Concurrency/replication: concurrency fuzz under ExpectedVersion::Any lets all N writers succeed concurrently with event numbers still gap-free and no WrongExpectedVersion; a follower receiving the same ReplicationEntry twice (duplicate AppendEntries) applies idempotently without regressing commit_index.
  • Pack/bench edge cases: PackWriter must stitch EventRecord frames across a segment-file boundary without gap or duplication; a crash mid-pack-export leaves a partial .esdbpack that pack verify flags IncompleteTrailer and pack import must abort before writing any event; a max_in_flight = 1 benchmark exercises the single-threaded subscribe baseline and must not deadlock even when acquire/release share a task; proptest shrinking on a found violation must terminate at a minimal (stream_len, snap_every) (bound input ranges).

14.9 Failure Analysis

Failure Detection Blast radius Containment Recovery
Replay-equivalence violation CI conformance test failure correctness of all snapshot-based aggregate loads block release; quarantine snapshot store identify non-deterministic handler; fix + regenerate fixtures
Raft safety violation fuzz test finds committed event missing post-heal entire cluster durability guarantee block release unconditionally audit log_matching property in esdb-replication; compare to Raft TLA+ spec
Crash-fuzz: missing committed event post-recovery event count < commit_index_before_kill data durability guarantee block release audit fsync path in esdb-log; verify O_DSYNC or explicit fsync after each batch
Pack import corruption CRC error during import verify step DR bootstrap fails import aborts; source cluster unaffected re-export from source; check destination disk health
Benchmark regression > 20% p99 CI bench comparison alert user-facing latency flag in PR; block merge until explained flamegraph diff vs baseline commit; bisect with git bisect run cargo bench
loom violation (data race) loom model panics with thread interleaving trace correctness of in-process concurrency block release trace shows exact interleaving; fix synchronization; re-run loom

14.10 Operational Notes

  • CI: replay-equivalence, crash-fuzz, and concurrency-fuzz run on every PR (< 5 min each); Raft safety fuzz and loom run nightly (slower); cargo test --test conformance -- --format json > conformance_report.json output is attached to release notes.
  • Benchmarking: bench_report.json is stored as a CI artifact per commit and auto-compared against the main tip, alerting on > 10% p99 regression or > 5% throughput regression; cargo flamegraph --bench bench_append_sequential -- --bench SVG output is committed to docs/perf/<version>/ and diffed on milestone releases.
  • DR/backup: runbook exports weekly to object storage (esdb-cli pack export --to HEAD), tests import quarterly on a throwaway node, and verifies with pack verify before storing; all failing fuzz seeds are checked into fixtures/ as deterministic regression guards, with a new seed added immediately on any CI failure.
  • Metrics (CI artifact, not runtime): replay_equiv_check_total, fuzz_violation_total{family}, pack_export_bytes_total, pack_export_duration_seconds, bench_append_p99_ms, bench_read_mbps. Release gate: all four conformance families green, all benchmarks within 10% of baseline, and pack round-trip passing, before any v1.0 tag.

Threat Model

Trust boundaries: (1) clients → esdb-server over TLS/mTLS (auth boundary); (2) esdb-server → in-process crates esdb-stream / esdb-log / esdb-replication (trusted, same process); (3) cluster node ↔ cluster node via mutual TLS (node-identity boundary); (4) operator → esdb-cli over local UNIX socket or loopback (operator-level trust, audited); (5) OS process → disk (physical access = ultimate root of trust; disk contents considered trusted only after CRC verification). The architecture diagram's esdb-partition routing layer is inside boundary 2; esdb-storage scavenge is an operator-privileged operation in boundary 4.

Threat surface summary by component:

Component External Trust Boundary Threat Exposure
esdb-server gRPC/HTTP Clients (boundary 1) Auth bypass, oversized payload, connection flood, replay attack
esdb-replication Raft RPC Cluster peers (boundary 3) Rogue node injection, split-brain, term manipulation
esdb-log segments OS / disk (boundary 5) CRC corruption, torn write, bit-flip, disk substitution
esdb-storage scavenge Operator (boundary 4) Irreversible data loss, wrong-stream targeting
esdb-projection handler Application code (untrusted) Panic, non-determinism, OOM, infinite loop
esdb-subscribe Consumers (boundary 1 extended) Subscription flood, slow consumer, ack manipulation
esdb-snapshot Application code + disk Schema poisoning, corrupt state Bytes

Attack Surface — Threat Table

ID Threat Surface Attack Vector Mitigation Detection Signal Telemetry Recovery Path
T01 Unauthorized append / read gRPC/HTTP endpoint Missing or stolen bearer JWT / client cert mTLS per cluster node; JWT with short TTL per client; per-stream/category ACL in esdb-server Auth failure counter esdb_auth_failures_total{peer_ip,operation} Revoke token; block IP via rate-limit rule (RB-08)
T02 Payload bomb — oversized event AppendEvents RPC body event.data > max configured size (default 4 MB/event, 64 MB/batch) Reject at ingress before deserialization; configurable max_event_bytes / max_batch_bytes RESOURCE_EXHAUSTED responses esdb_oversized_rejected_total No state written; return gRPC RESOURCE_EXHAUSTED; caller throttled
T03 Malformed record framing / CRC corruption esdb-log segment on disk Process kill mid-write; disk bit-flip; NFS re-order CRC32C per length-prefixed record (Phase 2, P8); torn-tail scan on startup; I4 atomicity CRC mismatch on scan esdb_crc_errors_total{node,segment} Truncate to last valid record; rebuild index; alert P1 (RB-07)
T04 OCC abuse — version-conflict flood esdb-stream append path Flood of appends with wrong ExpectedVersion to a hot stream Per-stream write rate-limit; WrongExpectedVersion counted; circuit-breaker in esdb-server Rate spike on OCC metric esdb_wrong_expected_version_total{stream_id} Caller back-off; circuit-breaker opens; I5 prevents any write
T05 Subscription flood / slow-consumer amplification esdb-subscribe Open unbounded persistent subscriptions; consumer processes at 0 bytes/s Max subscriptions per connection; backpressure token window (Phase 13); park queue size cap Queue depth histogram esdb_sub_pending_events_total{subscription_id} Park consumer; nack+retry with exponential back-off; drop if park full
T06 Rogue node — Raft log injection esdb-replication AppendEntries Forged RPC from a node not in membership Mutual TLS with cluster CA; membership list checked per message; term/index consistency check (Phase 10) Unexpected peer in Raft log esdb_raft_unknown_peer_total Reject RPC; log peer identity; mTLS prevents impersonation
T07 Split-brain via symmetric partition esdb-replication Network partition splits cluster into two equal halves Strict quorum requirement; Raft never commits without majority; I7 Two nodes emit Leader role metric esdb_raft_role cardinality; term mismatch Restore network; stale leader steps down on term bump
T08 Snapshot poisoning esdb-snapshot store Upload corrupt or schema-mismatched snapshot Snapshot has CRC + schema_version field; mismatch → reject and fall back to full replay (I9, Phase 6) Snapshot load failure esdb_snapshot_load_failures_total{stream} Delete bad snapshot; perform full stream replay; write new snapshot
T09 Scavenge-induced irreversible data loss esdb-storage scavenge Operator triggers scavenge on a stream without prior soft-delete Scavenge requires explicit tombstone first; mandatory dry-run output (ScavengePlan written to disk); confirmation echo Scavenge audit log esdb_scavenge_bytes_removed_total; ScavengePlan artifact Restore from pre-scavenge backup; scavenge is irreversible (RB-10)
T10 Index rebuild DoS esdb-storage recovery Adversary or flapping node repeatedly crashes to force expensive index rebuild Rate-limit restart attempts; persistent index checkpoint; partial rebuild from checkpoint (Phase 9) Startup duration spike esdb_index_rebuild_seconds{node} Restore from backup snapshot; cap restart rate via systemd StartLimitBurst
T11 Projection handler panic / isolation break esdb-projection Bug in handler or adversarial event payload triggers panic Each projection runs in isolated tokio::task; panic caught and projection marked Faulted; server continues (Phase 8) Fault count increase esdb_projection_faults_total{projection} Fix handler; rebuild from checkpoint (esdb-cli project rebuild) (RB-06)
T12 Memory exhaustion via unbounded read esdb-server read path ReadAll with count = u64::MAX Streaming cursor (never fully buffered); enforce max_read_batch per request; flow control RSS growth; heap pressure esdb_read_bytes_total; process RSS Drop connection; return RESOURCE_EXHAUSTED; no state corruption
T13 Supply-chain (malicious cargo dependency) Build pipeline Malicious crate published to crates.io cargo-deny allowlist; lock file committed; cargo audit in CI; vendor in vendor/ for air-gapped builds CI policy gate fails SCA alert blocks build Pin hash; vendor crates; incident response on confirmed compromise
T14 Operator error — delete wrong stream CLI Admin API esdb-cli stream delete <wrong-id> typo Soft-delete creates tombstone; scavenge is a separate explicit step; CLI echoes stream-id for confirmation Audit log entry esdb_stream_tombstones_total Restore from backup before scavenge runs (RB-13)
